# Session Log — 2026-09

Append-only. Newest entry at the bottom. One entry per session, written by
the chronicler BEFORE the session's final commit so the entry rides in it.
Format: `## <date> — <issue/trigger> — <outcome>`, then what was
attempted/shipped (commit hash), ratchet movement, decisions, surprises,
friction, next.

## 2026-09-01 — #815 — **the tree's fourteen drifted `GAP(...)` owner citations agree with the queue again under one convention per direction — ten repointed at issues open today, one marker DELETED outright, three ruled unowned in prose naming no `#N` — and against `origin/main` `gapaudit` reads census 67 → 66, group 1 28 → 17, group 2 34 → 25**: STYLE P3 makes a marker's `#N` an ownership claim and a closed one a dead end, and fourteen markers across eight files had drifted off that — six said *"no issue owns this"* while a live open issue already did, eight cited only a now-closed issue — so `gapaudit` reported the marker untracked in group 1 while its owner sat in group 2, which is verbatim the pairing the issue title names; `Spec: n/a` is not an omission but the oracle's finding, since STYLE P3 gives a marker an area tag *"never a rule ID or clause"* and all five spec files return **zero matches for "GAP"**; the arbiter **REJECTED** round 1 on two blocking findings that are both *measurement-instrument* defects rather than judgment errors about ownership — a citation placed after the marker's bullet list, which `tools/gapaudit`'s `paragraph()` never reaches, and a no-owner rewrite naming OPEN #499/#578 while disclaiming them, which its bare `#(\d+)` `citations` scan cannot tell from an ownership claim — each moving the audit the wrong way in **opposite** directions, so the repair's totals are **unchanged at census 66 / group 1 17 / group 2 25** and the **four-row membership diff is the whole measurement**; the arbiter agreed with all three NO OWNER rulings, with the deletion, and raised no finding against any of the ten repoints, and it found the two defects only by taking the reconciliation measurement mason had skipped *"for want of an issue list on stdin"* in a container where docs/ROUTINES.md's **Survey input** recipe works; **`Ratchet: unchanged`** run rather than inferred, `surface: unchanged`, 8 files **+31/−32**, all `.go`, and `git diff origin/main...HEAD -U0` filtered to anything not starting `//` is **empty across both commits** — not one executable line moved.

- Shipped on `wip/issue-815`, base `origin/main` at **`055aed7`**, unmoved
  through grounding, both implementation rounds and both verdicts — no merge
  forward, no re-judgment on a moved base. Two commits: **`c5b2e223`**
  (`docs: repoint drifted GAP marker owners (#815)`, rejected, 8 files,
  +29/−28) and **`627ed250`** (`docs: fix GAP marker repoint boundary and
  disclaimer citations (#815)`, the repair, 3 files / 4 hunks, +11/−13 on
  top; nothing amended, nothing force-pushed). Branch total from `055aed7`:
  **8 files, +31/−32** — `parser/produce_complex.go`, `validate/cvcelt.go`,
  `validate/icpath.go`, `xsd/contentmatcher.go`, `xsd/contentrestricts.go`,
  `xsd/defaultbinding.go`, `xsd/redefinition.go`, `xsd/wildcard.go`. This
  entry rides the landing. **#815 closes.**
- **Ratchet: unchanged.** The arbiter's figures, copied not recomputed:
  `GOXSD_RATCHET=1 go test ./conformance -run TestConformance -count=1` exit 0
  (**310.58s**) on the accepted commit, then **immediately**
  `git status --porcelain -- conformance/testdata/expectations/` — **empty**,
  full tree clean after. No lane moved, nothing to bank, no removals to
  assert; the round-1 write-run on `c5b2e223` had already found the same.
  Gate part 4 at the accepted commit **PASS (306.44s)** — **41759 cases across
  6 lanes, 99 withheld as inapplicable**, no improved-but-unbanked case
  surfaced, declined counts at the standing baseline (**datatypes 8, schema
  196 + 11, instance 15315 + 5**), the identical census all four rounds
  reported. Parts 1–3 clean at both commits: build/test/vet exit 0 with every
  package `ok` and `go vet` silent, `go tool lint` `0 issues.`,
  `go tool commentwrap ./...` clean with no reflow needed (`-fix` not
  required at either commit). `go tool surface -base origin/main` →
  **`surface: unchanged`** (T5) at both, which zero touched exported
  identifiers makes structural, so no warden pass was owed. Tests that cannot
  fail: not applicable — no test file and no executable line changed, so
  there is no test whose reversion could be checked.
- Cost — **1 oracle, 2 mason (1 repair), 2 arbiter (1 reject).** No warden
  (`surface: unchanged`, confirmed by outcome), no cartographer, no steward.
  **What the reject bought is the acceptance measurement itself, not a change
  of judgment.** Round 1 got every ownership call right — the arbiter agreed
  with all three NO OWNER rulings (one on strictly better evidence than
  mason's), agreed the deletion was faithful, and raised no finding against
  any of the ten repoints — and still shipped two markers that were wrong
  *to the tool* while reading correctly to a human. The arbiter built a
  661-issue feed from docs/ROUTINES.md's **Survey input** recipe, ran
  `gapaudit` on `HEAD` and on `origin/main` against one identical canonical
  feed, and read the two defects straight off group membership. The accepting
  round then rebuilt that feed **from scratch** — 12 pages, 661 issues, 252
  open, fed whole rather than filtered to `kind/gap` — and audited three
  trees materialised with `git archive` so that only the tree varies,
  reproducing the account's table and its four-row membership diff
  independently rather than taking either.
- Decisions made and why:
  - **One editing pass, one convention per direction — not section A now and
    section B's three undecided rows later.** Those three rows are decidable
    only by reading the same successor issues section B already names, so
    splitting buys a second grounding round and leaves the tree carrying two
    wordings for one ruling in the interval. The issue's own Notes say *"One
    landing, one convention — do not split."*
  - **The three "to be decided" rows are ruled NO OWNER, and the repoint
    alternative is affirmatively wrong rather than merely weaker.** #345's own
    thread disclaims `defaultbinding.go`'s clause-4.2 site in the owner's own
    words and covers the clause-5.2.2 twin in one further sentence; #372, the
    issue that residue passed to, then closed `completed`. For
    `contentrestricts.go`'s `maxContentPositions` row, #499 is
    `maxProductStates` — a different function, a different constant, a later
    phase — and #578's Spec section reads *"no spec rule is violated and no
    verdict is wrong … this is a cost defect"*, so it cannot own a declined
    verdict. #267, #413, #584 and #774 were each ruled out by name.
  - **The no-owner statement names no `#N` at all, rather than being moved out
    of the marker's paragraph.** Both repairs satisfy `gapaudit`; only
    dropping the numbers also reads correctly to a cold reader, because
    relocating the sentence re-creates finding 1's shape at a new site. The
    three reasons are given by description, and the reasoning that names them
    is on the thread, which is where the verdict rules it belongs.
  - **`#501` at `contentrestricts.go:634` is left standing.** It is the
    reproduction reference for a demonstrated false reject, not a retirement
    claim, and being closed it cannot make the marker read as tracked — it is
    what keeps that row's `dead end: cites CLOSED #501` annotation correct.
    The Acceptance's *"without a `#N`"* clause governs the ownership sentence,
    and that sentence has none.
  - **The deletion was applied from the build spec, not re-derived.** The
    2026-08-31 post-land comment on the thread ruled `produce_complex.go:2350`
    a DELETION rather than a repoint; mason verified the two facts it rests on
    (`xsd/attributewildcardfold.go` exists, and `xsd/resolve.go:247` runs
    `foldAttributeWildcards` immediately before `checkComplexDerivations` at
    `:250`) and folded the one surviving true sentence into the
    `{attribute uses}` / **#401** paragraph directly above it as its genuine
    twin, citing **#414**. The literal string `GAP(` was kept out of the
    replacement prose on purpose so the debt sweep does not re-acquire the
    site, and the `#414` is not a marker citation, so P3 does not reach it.
  - **The two `defaultbinding.go` `#236`/`#372` touch-ups were taken inside
    the repair round, not deferred.** Mason asked for this to be scrutinised
    and two independent grounds settle it: they are two of the issue's own
    three "to be decided" rows and were never outside the fourteen, and round
    1's repair instruction named them verbatim. Leaving them would have landed
    two wordings for one ruling; measured, the
    `dead end: cites CLOSED #236` / `#372` annotation pairs go **2 → 0**.
- Surprises:
  - **Both blocking defects are invisible to a prose read and visible only to
    the instrument.** `bulletPattern` and a blank comment line are hard
    paragraph boundaries in `tools/gapaudit`, whose `marker` type states the
    contract outright — *"[paragraph]'s boundary rules decide what counts as
    cited"* — so a citation placed after a marker's bullets is not in the
    marker's text at all; and `citations` is a bare `#(\d+)` scan whose own
    doc says there is *"no signal here to tell the two apart"*. The first left
    `validate/cvcelt.go:154` in group 1 with **#1119 still in group 2**, the
    exact pathology the issue exists to fix; the second did the reverse,
    silencing a correct group-1 row so a gap this landing rules unowned read
    as tracked, and pulling **#499 out of group 2** while #499's real marker
    stayed in group 1.
  - **The repair moves no total.** Census 66, group 1 17, group 2 25 before
    and after, because the two findings move in opposite directions and
    cancel. A totals-only check would have shown a clean repair as a no-op and
    the rejected commit as equally good; the membership diff — exactly four
    rows, and nothing else — is the only reading that distinguishes them.
  - **A fifteenth site carries finding 1's exact mechanism and is pre-existing
    on `main`.** Found by mason's own grep for surviving copies of the claim
    it was correcting, not by the audit.
  - **Mason's reason for leaving that fifteenth site was wrong even though the
    call was right.** The account argued that fixing it *"would change the very
    membership this repair round exists to demonstrate"*; the arbiter ruled
    that protecting a baseline is never grounds for leaving a defect, since a
    measurement is an observation of the tree and the tree owes it nothing.
    The sound reasons are that it is pre-existing on `main` and outside a
    repair instruction already written as closed — and that enlarging the ask
    at round 2 under a two-round cap is the failure the cap exists to prevent.
  - **Mason's reflow worry was already closed by #1117.**
    `hashEndsParagraph` returns **false** for a `#` immediately followed by
    digits, so a future `commentwrap` reflow moving `#1119` to a line start
    cannot drop it. The new mid-paragraph host is stable.
- Friction — **an issue whose acceptance measurement needs a survey feed does
  not say so, and round 1 shipped without the one instrument that could see
  its defects.** #815's Goal is *"so `go tool gapaudit` and a cold reader agree
  with the queue"*, which makes the group-1/group-2 reconciliation the
  acceptance measurement and not an optional extra — yet mason's round-1
  account records the groups as *"skipped for want of an issue list on
  stdin"*, in a container where the **Survey input** recipe works and returned
  661 issues for the arbiter minutes later. Neither the issue body, the
  grounding, nor `/develop` names the feed as the thing to build, so the
  measurement was skipped by the round that most needed it and then built
  three times over — once by the rejecting arbiter, once by the repairing
  mason, once from scratch by the accepting arbiter. CLAUDE.md already says
  both surveys read their issue list from stdin and ROUTINES already ranks the
  channels; what is missing is the instruction that an issue asking for
  agreement with the queue is measured that way. Routed below. Documented
  costs, one clause each: four full four-part gates plus two ratchet
  write-runs on a diff with zero executable lines — part 4 at 308.4s, 310.6s,
  325.6s and 306.44s plus the 310.58s write-run — which is the gate block
  working as the only definition of the gate and is **already a queued
  `/retro` question** raised by #1145's landing the day before, so this
  session adds a second data point and not a new finding; the submodule init
  per fresh worktree (#659); and the issue body's line numbers drifting under
  the branch, relayed to #267 and #345 as thread comments rather than body
  edits, which is #764's convention working.
- Next — nothing is discharged on the branch; all of it is owed to the
  post-land pass, which disposes of these and does not merely record them:

  2. **File the fifteenth site** (cartographer):

     **#499** in a sentence at **`:787`**, several blank comment lines below

  3. **File the sixteenth site** (cartographer):
     `xsd/contentrestricts.go:1047`, the element-side `key-dft-binding` cases

  4. **A question for `/retro`, with the Friction above as its evidence**:

  5. **No body edits are owed on #267 or #345.** The line-drift corrections

## 2026-09-01 — post-land pass for #815

**ZERO unblocked and the zero is measured; TWO issues filed — #1156 for BOTH
remaining `contentrestricts.go` marker sites in one landing rather than the two
the `Next` list asked for, and #1157 for the process half that bought this
issue's reject round; ONE ownership dispute RULED (`contentrestricts.go:1047`
belongs to #345, not #267, and both threads carry it); FOUR bodies corrected
where this landing falsified a premise — which is one more disposition than the
landing entry's `Next` item 5 expected, and the reason is that line drift and a
falsified premise are different defects.** Six advisory items dismissed with the
reason and, where one exists, the trigger that would reverse it.
`docs/PLAN.md` untouched, deliberately.

- **Unblock sweep: ZERO, measured.** **No open issue's `## Depends on` names
  #815**, and none of the **20** open `blocked` issues mentions it in one. Feed
  taken with the recipe as `fe40c87` left it — `state=all`, 12 pages, page 12
  short at 55 and re-requested per #1145, **661** non-PR issues, **251 open**,
  matching the arbiter's own landing-time feed at 661/252 with #815's closure the
  single difference. Exactly five open bodies contain `#815` — **#1148**,
  **#1119**, **#1061**, **#871**, **#825** — every one citing it as prior art or
  as an adjacent reading; the only `blocked` one, #871, waits on **#831**. #815
  was filed `Depends on: none` and nothing was ever queued behind it. Written
  down because a measured zero and a skipped sweep are otherwise
  indistinguishable from `git log` (#400). `wip/issue-815` is gone from the
  remote, as a merged lease should be.
- **The audit re-run reproduces the arbiter's landed figures exactly**, which is
  what makes the two filings measurable rather than asserted: `go tool gapaudit`
  on `main` at `93cd789` against that feed reports census **66**, group 1 **17**,
  group 2 **25**.
- **#1156 FILED — `kind/refactor`, `ready`, `area/xsd`: `Next` items 2 and 3 are
  ONE issue, not two.** Both are one-line comment edits in one file, both make
  `gapaudit`'s owner join work, and both are the same acceptance measurement, so
  splitting them buys a second four-part gate (part 4 runs ~310 s) for a diff
  with zero executable lines. That is #815's own *"One landing, one convention —
  do not split."* applied to its own residue. Line numbers re-verified against
  the landed tree rather than carried from the thread, which quotes the
  pre-branch `:1043`.
  - **Row 1, `:742` `maxProductStates`** — cites OPEN **#499** in a sentence at
    `:787`, below the head paragraph and three blank comment lines, so
    `paragraph()` never reaches it. Confirmed a group-1 row **and #499 confirmed
    a group-2 row**: both halves of this issue's title pathology, at a fifteenth
    site. Its Acceptance keeps the retirement CONDITION at the site — that text
    is #499's option (b) substance — and moves only the token.
  - **Row 2, `:1047` `elementPositionBinding`** — names nobody, confirmed group
    1. **#345 is NOT in group 2**, because `defaultbinding.go:251` already cites
    it, so this row moves group 1 only; the Acceptance says that rather than
    predicting a group-2 drop the tool cannot deliver. The accept verdict's
    *"Both leave #499 in group 2"* is loose on this row and is corrected here.
  - **The alternative was ruled out at filing rather than left to the session:**
    widening `paragraph()` to swallow a whole comment block would tie a marker to
    every number below it, which is the failure this issue's own finding 2
    landed to prevent, and #1117 already settled the one boundary arm that was a
    defect.
- **RULED — `xsd/contentrestricts.go:1047` is owned by #345, not #267.** `Next`
  item 3 named #345's `## Spec`; it is not the only claim. **#267 absorbed the
  same marker by a scope-addition comment on 2026-07-27**
  (`issuecomment-5088516649`, from #263's post-land pass, quoting it verbatim)
  four days before #345 was filed, and `gapaudit` annotates the row with both
  numbers as candidate owners. Ruled for **#345** on two grounds: it is the later
  and twice-reaffirmed position (its body plus the 2026-08-01 sweep's
  `issuecomment-5151798594`), and its Acceptance cross-references this site **to**
  #267's case-3 site, which only parses if the two sites have two owners —
  citing #267 at both would make the marker's own *"element-side twin of
  `attributeDefaultBinding`'s case-3 marker"* sentence circular. Recorded on both
  threads and in both bodies, so the next reader does not re-derive it from the
  annotations.
- **#1157 FILED — `kind/process`, `ready`, `area/meta`: `Next` item 4, filed
  rather than handed to `/retro`.** *"Queued for `/retro`"* is not a ledger
  (#330); a filed issue is, and #1157 says in its own Notes that `/retro` may
  discharge it directly, which is #1140's model. **The sharper statement of the
  defect is that CLAUDE.md's own Surveys block is what misled**: *"Empty stdin is
  a supported mode, not a failure"* is true for a census and false for an
  acceptance measurement, and nothing in front of the mason distinguished them.
  Cost priced from this landing rather than asserted — one reject round and one
  repair round on a zero-executable-line diff, a fourth four-part gate at 325.6 s
  plus a second ratchet write-run, to move four rows of a table round 1 could
  have printed. Four adjacent issues checked and each ruled not this: **#1140**
  (that mason ran the recipe and misread an auth error; this one never ran it),
  **#1153**, **#692** (the closest sibling by mechanism — an expectation in a
  body does not bind a session — and #1157 requires it be read first and its
  ruling reused rather than a second spelling of the same answer), **#510**.
- **FOUR body corrections, against `Next` item 5's expectation of none.** That
  item was right about **line drift** — already commented on #267 and #345, and a
  third comment retires nothing. It does not reach a premise this landing made
  **false**, which docs/WORKFLOW.md's filing discipline fixes in the body. Each
  edit carries a comment saying what moved and what did not.
  - **#267** — title and `## Current state` said the marker was *untracked* and
    at `:81`; it is at `:90` and reads `#267 owns the retirement.` Repoint bullet

    the issue is one site again. The substantive `key-governing-ad` question is
    untouched and it stays `blocked` on #250.
  - **#345** — Acceptance said *"Today all three name nothing"*; re-scoped to the
    **two** sites it owns, with the clause-4.2 site struck rather than deleted so
    it is not re-derived, the line numbers corrected in place, and the twin
    cross-reference bullet struck as discharged by this landing.
  - **#1119** — Goal and two Acceptance bullets turned on the string *"No issue
    owns this residual yet."*, which no longer exists in the tree. Struck as
    discharged, and the head-paragraph placement constraint recorded, since a

  - **#499** — found in passing, and stale for longer than this landing: the body
    still said *"Today the marker cites #282, which is closed"* and put the marker

- **DISMISSED, with reasons.** **The three no-owner markers**
  (`contentrestricts.go:629`, `defaultbinding.go:345`, `defaultbinding.go:550`)
  are the landing's ruled and accepted output, not a debt it created, and they
  are correctly in group 1 — which is what group 1 means for an unowned gap.
  `gapaudit`'s group 1 is a mechanically re-checkable ledger for them, so filing
  three issues whose Acceptance is *"someone decide"* would add nothing the
  survey does not already print; #1156 says explicitly not to touch them.
  **`#501` at `:634`** stays: a reproduction reference, ruled *"no change
  wanted"* in round 1, and that reading belongs to open **#1061**. **Mason's two
  scrutiny items** — cold-reader reachability of the descriptive no-owner form,
  and whether a `commentwrap` reflow can strand `#1119` at a line start — were
  both ruled in the accept verdict against the source, the second against
  `hashEndsParagraph` directly, and nothing is left to track. **The gate-cost
  friction** is already a queued `/retro` question from #1145's landing the day
  before; this landing is its second data point, not a second defect.
- **`docs/PLAN.md` untouched, and deliberately.** Its Status section carries #815
  at row 3 and the now-closed #1145 at row 2 of the top band, both stale. Step 6
  replaces that section **whole or not at all** — a fresh `lanestatus` paste, a
  fresh namespace survey, fresh milestone and queue counts — which is a

**`15420` is not an instrument's output at all** but `15561 − 141`, a decline-census baseline minus the count of expectation-line flips the ratchet wrote, against two baselines #853's own thread quotes incompatibly (`15561` round 1, `15569` at `c59e993` round 2) — and under BOTH readings no set of cases corresponds to it, which is a stronger answer than picking one; - Decisions made and why:
  - **The expectation-file census is the authoritative figure, and the pick
    was verified rather than asserted.** It is the only lane figure the
    ratchet writes, the only one a later session can re-derive from a
    committed tree with neither the W3C submodule nor a ~400s run, and the
    object CLAUDE.md's one rule is already stated over. Its known cost is
    that on a branch mid-landing it understates the engine by exactly the
    improved-but-unbanked count — which is the score being what is BANKED —
    and the arbiter ruled WORKFLOW's pre-existing *"states the delta alone"*
    sentence already covers that writer, since a writer with pending movement
    has a delta and no bank by definition.
  - **The 15420 classification claims only what #853's thread proves.** Mason
    established `15420 = 15561 − 141`, that no run printed it, and that the
    thread quotes two incompatible baselines — then explicitly declined to
    rule which was real, and asked the arbiter to check that the 8-case
    reading (`15569 − 141 = 15428` reproduces the measurement, so 8 of the 141
    improved cases may have been decided-and-wrong rather than declined) had
    not been smuggled in as established. It had not: it is offered as one of
    two and asserted as neither. The operation is also **commit-specific and
    cannot be re-derived at HEAD** — it needs an in-flight landing's banked
    flip count, and at HEAD there is none.
  - **The runner does NOT also print the committed file census beside the
    decline census.** That is a second instrument emitting the score, which is
    the exact duplication that produced the phantom figure in the first place
    (STYLE D3).
  - **The full four-way partition is not printed on every run.** Computing
    decided-wrong inside `reportDeclines` means comparing against the
    committed file from a census that deliberately takes only `cases` and
    `actual` and scores nothing, and it prints MORE non-authoritative numbers
    beside the one people misquote. If it is wanted it is its own issue.
  - **The ruling lives in `docs/WORKFLOW.md`, not in
    `conformance/testdata/expectations/README.md`.** CLAUDE.md's table gives
    WORKFLOW.md the rules every session obeys, and this governs what a
    `docs/LOG` entry and a `Ratchet:` trailer quote.

    from those paragraphs, and that is acceptable** because `docs/PLAN.md:22`
    still says *"paste `go tool lanestatus` verbatim"*, so a reader landing on

  1173, schema 13830 / 1568 / 15398, instance 11029 / 15332 / 26361**;
  `ber`, `json` and `xpath` empty.
- Cost — **1 oracle, 2 mason (0 repair), 1 arbiter (ACCEPT round 1, 0 rejects).**
  No warden (`surface: unchanged`), no cartographer, no steward. The two mason
  rounds are two ACCOUNTS OF ONE COMMIT, not an implementation and a repair:
  `0572ffb` is unchanged between them and the second supersedes the first in
  words, paying a second full four-part gate on a tree that had not moved
  (conformance 452.49s then 466.79s). **The oracle's finding was that it had
  nothing to find**, and the useful half is what it ruled out: `Spec: n/a` is
  established, not asserted, and the trap it named — *"read PLAN.md, do not
  infer"* the milestone per package, because the issue's own M9/M10
  parenthetical is illustrative wording and not a sourced fact — is the one
  mistake this landing could have made silently. The grounding also proved the
  premise still held (`go doc -all` → **zero** `func`/`type`/`var`/`const` lines
  in each of the three packages), found no duplicate in the open queue with
  #1003 and #1123 confirmed adjacent-not-owning, and established no overlapping
  in-flight work from `git log` over the four files showing exactly one commit
  ever — **`f429dd2`, the original authoring — nothing had touched them since.**
- Decisions made and why:
  - **The heading route for both files, and the same route for both.** The
    issue's explicit instruction was not to leave one file in each style;
    `xsd/doc.go`'s Walker section and `parser/doc.go`'s instance-hint reader
    are the module's demonstrated answer to this exact question and both keep
    the signatures visible, so the heading preserves the shape a reader came
    for while the prose route would have destroyed it.
  - **`codegen`'s asymmetry is deliberate: one lead-in sentence covers three
    sections instead of three more tags.** The sentence renders directly under
    the package summary and ahead of every heading, and the sections that carry
    the hedge in their own headings are the ones describing *surface* — the
    entry point and the Emitter seam — while Determinism, Type narrowing and
    Naming describe design properties. Mason flagged this as the thing it most
    wanted overruled; the arbiter ruled it correct on T6's terms plus
    CLAUDE.md's *"state it once"*.
  - **`# Planned entry point (M9 — not yet implemented)` discards the old
    heading's words and keeps them as the section's first line**, which is
    T6's own *"relabel it planned, don't delete it"* and keeps the grep words
    in the normative sentence.
  - **`bersrc` took the optional-clause form** — `# Planned mapping (M11 — not
    yet implemented; contract fixed now)`, `builtin/native/doc.go:4`'s spelling
    — preserving exactly what the struck parenthetical claimed. Its inclusion
    was mason's other flagged doubt, since `/backlog` struck it twice
    (2026-08-16, 2026-08-17) before the 2026-08-27 re-measurement reinstated
    it; the body and the GROUNDING both include it, and the arbiter agreed.
  - **One item absorbed, in the same file and the same defect class**:
    `codegen`'s Emitter-seam heading claimed `API frozen in M9` over a
    `value.Emitter` that `value/doc.go:192` itself records as *"not yet
    declared here"*. The committed heading takes that sibling's spelling
    **verbatim, semicolon and "here" included**, which is what keeps it from
    being a seventh spelling. In scope per WORKFLOW's absorption rule — no rule
    ID outside the grounding, no exported surface, no lane movement — named in
    the commit body rather than filed as a new issue.
  - **Four things checked and deliberately NOT absorbed**, each with its
    reason recorded on the thread rather than left silent: the root list's
    `validate` bullet, because `validate/doc.go:13-15` owns adapter status and
    states it truthfully with milestones and CLAUDE.md's ownership rule forbids
    the root doc restating it; `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:541`, which mirrors
    `codec`'s old heading but already hedges correctly and whose
    cross-reference still resolves; `docs/PLAN.md:267-268`, which quotes the
    old `codegen/doc.go:46` heading **inside a dated evidence stamp** — a
    record of what the tree said, and rewriting it would falsify the stamp;
    and `README.md` plus `codegen.Option`, both fenced out by the thread's own
    standing instructions (#1003 owns the README layer).
- Surprises:
  - **All three of mason's scrutiny requests resolved in mason's favour and
    the verdict carried zero findings — blocking or advisory.** That is rare
    enough in this log to be worth the line, and it is not the same thing as a
    thin review: the arbiter re-ran the whole gate, ran the ratchet itself,
    re-read `docs/PLAN.md`'s milestone sections fresh rather than checking them
    against the issue's parenthetical, and re-verified the export-zero premise
    at judgment time.
  - **Mason's own account misquotes its own edit.** The account writes the
    Emitter-seam heading as `…; API frozen in M9, not yet declared)`; the
    committed line is `…; API frozen in M9; not yet declared here)`, the better
    one. The arbiter recorded it as a process note and explicitly not a finding
    against the diff, on the grounds that the diff is what lands. Same shape as
    #1120's landing, where mason corrected a false claim in its own round-1
    account — an account that cannot be edited into truth is superseded in
    words.
  - **`go test ./...` was killed by the container with exit 144, twice, mid-run**
    under mason, with no FAIL line in either partial log; mason completed the
    gate by splitting it into three foreground chunks, and the arbiter's single
    combined invocation then completed cleanly at 564.35s. Container variance,
    first sighting in this log, coverage real either way — recorded, not routed.
  - **The four files had not been touched since the commit that created them.**
    A doc-accuracy defect in a file nobody edits does not decay and does not
    surface itself; it took nine consumer sightings to move.
- Friction — **the issue was filed 2026-08-02 and landed today, and in the
  interval nine INDEPENDENT libuser sightings reported its `codegen`/`codec`
  half as a new finding, across twelve consecutive persona consultations.**
  Every one was recorded on the thread rather than filed as a duplicate, which

  `ready` issue reconfirmed nine times by the one persona that can see the
  defect is a queue-ordering signal that the ready queue has no way to carry,
  and the same issue was re-scoped twice on the way (three packages struck as
  their premise expired, `bersrc` struck twice and reinstated once). **Two
  sessions is a pattern and this is one issue, so it is recorded and not
  routed** — `/retro` counts sightings-before-pick if a second issue shows the

  bought nothing, because it re-ran an unchanged tree for a second mason
  account.**
- **One watch-item, recorded with its trigger and deliberately NOT filed.** The
  arbiter named it and declined to make it a finding: `codegen/doc.go`'s

- Next — nothing further is discharged on the branch; the rest is owed to the

## 2026-09-01 — post-land pass for #409

**ZERO unblocked and the zero is measured; ZERO issues filed; ONE watch-item
DISMISSED with both arms of its recorded reversal trigger measured cold rather
than assumed, and the trigger SHARPENED into a form the next pass re-tests in
one grep; ONE friction pattern confirmed still unrouted and not duplicated
here.** No issue body needed correcting — this landing falsified no premise
anywhere in the queue, which is what a doc-only change with no exported-surface
delta should do and is written down because a checked-and-empty result and a
skipped check are otherwise indistinguishable from `git log` (#400).

- **Unblock sweep: ZERO, measured.** **No open issue's `## Depends on` names
  #409**, and none of the **20** open `blocked` issues mentions it in one. Feed
  taken from the REST issues endpoint after the merge per docs/ROUTINES.md's
  **Survey input** recipe — 12 pages at `per_page=100`, every page but the last
  full, **1162** items reshaping to **664** non-PR issues of which **252** are
  open. Sixteen open bodies contain the digits `409`; **eleven carry a real
  `#409` reference and every one of them is an explicit non-dependency** —
  #1144, #1123, #1003, #896, #748, #671, #670, #625, #513, #492, #472, all
  filing it as *adjacent*, *related-not-gating*, or (in #896's case) *"Not
  #409"*. Two of those state it inside the `## Depends on` section itself and
  both negate it in the same sentence: #492's *"none. (Adjacent, not gating:
  … **#409**, the doc.go present-tense contract sweep …)"* and #472's
  *"**none.** … Related, not gating: … **#409**"*. The remaining five matches
  are not references at all — `#1073` cites a comment URL ending `…5440931409`,
  #578/#499/#363 cite the constant `4096`, #768 cites commit `409fda3`, #748
  cites `+9409`. #409 was filed `Depends on: none` and nothing was ever queued
  behind it. `wip/issue-409` is gone from the remote, as a merged lease should
  be — 10 heads remain.
- **The arbiter's `# Determinism` watch-item: DISMISSED, and the dismissal is
  a measurement, not a judgment call.** The landing entry recorded the item with
  a two-arm reversal trigger; both arms were tested at `c5b789b`.
  - **Arm 2 (`codegen` grows tests) — cold.** `go list -f
    '{{len .TestGoFiles}}{{len .XTestGoFiles}}' ./codegen` prints `00`; the
    package is still one file.
  - **Arm 1 (a second present-tense claim under a lead-in) — cold, on a
    BOUNDED sweep.** The hazard is a claim a reader can falsify from the repo,
    so the candidate set is the module's **eleven zero-test packages**, not
    every present-tense sentence in the tree. Two near-misses, both exempt for
    the same reason: `codec/doc.go:21-23` (*"Differential tests feed identical
    input to both … benchmarks pin the fast path's budget"*) is a repo-state
    claim and false today, but stands under the hedged **heading** `# Planned
    decode paths, one semantics (M10 — not yet implemented)`, which is the
    compliant form arm 1 exempts by name; `builtin/native/doc.go:17-19` and
    `:25-27` stand under `# Planned representations (M12 — …)` and are not even
    false, since `value/backendtest` exists. The other eight zero-test packages
    make no test claim at all.
  - **The finding that makes arm 1 cheap from now on: only two files in the
    tree carry the lead-in form, and they are the two #409 touched.** `codec`'s
    lead-in is redundant — both its sections carry hedged headings
    independently — so **`codegen/doc.go` is the only file in the module where
    an unhedged heading stands under a lead-in**, and within its three such
    sections *"Golden-file tests pin the output"* is the only sentence
    falsifiable from the repo rather than from the future. Everything else
    there describes an emitter or a namer that does not exist, which the
    lead-in's *"every section below states the M9 contract"* covers exactly.
  - **On the merits as well.** The defect #409 closed was real-looking
    *signatures* in code blocks — `Generate`, `Target`, `AppendCanonical` —
    which is what all nine libuser sightings named, every one; across twelve
    consecutive consultations this sentence drew **zero**. Filing it would file
    against a trigger its own author had not seen fire.
  - **The trigger is restated on the thread, with a third and cheapest arm
    added**: if any future edit touches `codegen/doc.go` for another reason,
    correct the sentence in that landing rather than filing for it. A one-clause
    fix riding an existing diff costs nothing; a doc-only issue for it costs a
    full four-part gate, and this landing already spent ~28 minutes of
    conformance on four `doc.go` files with zero executable lines.
- **The nine-sighting friction pattern: confirmed NOT routed here, and not
  duplicated.** The landing entry rules it a `/retro` counting job — *"two
  sessions is a pattern and this is one issue"* — and this pass files nothing
  for it. Verified rather than asserted: no open issue in the 252 names
  sightings-before-pick or re-confirmation count as a queue-ordering signal.
  The nearest neighbours are process issues about a different failure each
  (#779 queue hygiene, #912 enumerated scope going stale, #1111 simultaneous
  claimability) and none of them would absorb it.
- The landing entry's `Next` list is fully discharged: 1 landed as `c5b789b`
  with `Closes #409.` as its own sentence, 2 is the measured zero above, and 3
  said there was nothing else — there was not. **This pass filed no issue and
  corrected no body, which is the correct output when a landing changes only
  prose in packages that export nothing.** `docs/PLAN.md` untouched: this
  landing moved no lane and no count in it, so there is nothing for a restamp
  to replace, and its Status data is the next `/backlog`'s by step 6's
  replacement rule. Thread record: this pass's own account is
  `kud360/goxsd8#409` `issuecomment-5492126260`, the only comment it wrote.

## 2026-09-01 — #1115 with #441 — **§3.2.1's simple-type-only typing of an Attribute Declaration's `{type definition}` is enforced at construction for the first time, so `ownedTypeFold`'s absence of attribute-side roots stops being a convention and becomes a consequence — the `GAP(xsd)` marker and both paragraphs arguing the unenforced state DELETE, and `gapaudit`'s census reads 66 → 65**: `xsd.NewAttributeDeclaration` now rejects an `InlineTypeDefinition` wrapping a `ComplexType` and charges **`a-props-correct` clause 1**, deliberately not `xsderr.RuleComponentInvariant` — the element side's twin rejection stays `RuleComponentInvariant` because THERE the shape is legal and only the entry point is wrong, and one test table drives both refs through both constructors so the asymmetry is pinned in one place; the oracle's finding is the one that settles the design fork #1115's body left open — **option (b) (widen `ownedTypeFold`'s roots to the attribute side) is not merely costlier than (a), it is spec-FORECLOSED by it**, since §3.4.2.4 clause 3 and §3.4.2.5 clause 2 read `{attribute uses}`/`{attribute wildcard}` and never an attribute declaration's own type slot's variety, so once (a) holds there is nothing complex-shaped at that slot for a widened root to ever reach; the same grounding's queue search found **#441 — open since 2026-08-02, `ready`, `kind/bug`, no live branch — is the identical fix already fully specified, and specified BETTER** (it settles the rule ID and the placement that #1115 leaves implicit), so the two were absorbed into one landing rather than one of them being re-derived; the arbiter **REJECTED** round 1 on one finding and it is a defect of the SWEEP, not of the change — the stale-marker sweep found two of three cross-references and missed the third **in the same package as the deletion**, `ComplexType.AttributeUses`'s doc, which the arbiter found with the one `grep -rn ownedtypefold` the sweep should have run; **`Ratchet: unchanged`** run rather than inferred, lane totals an exact match across both rounds, `surface: unchanged` both rounds, 7 files **+110/−49**.

- Shipped on `wip/issue-1115`, base `origin/main` at **`4e1d49b`**, unmoved
  through grounding, both implementation rounds and both verdicts — no merge
  forward, no re-judgement on a moved base. Two commits: **`88ebe569`**
  (`xsd: NewAttributeDeclaration rejects an inline ComplexType at {type
  definition} (#441)`, rejected, 6 files, +107/−47, carrying both `Closes`
  sentences) and **`9f0ff3bf`** (`xsd: fix stale GAP marker reference in
  AttributeUses doc (#441, #1115)`, the repair, 1 file, +3/−2, no closing
  keyword of its own); nothing amended, nothing force-pushed. Branch total
  from `4e1d49b`: **7 files, +110/−49** — `xsd/attributedeclaration.go`,
  `xsd/typedefinition.go`, `xsd/typedefinition_test.go`,
  `xsd/ownedtypefold.go`, `xsd/complextype.go`, `parser/produce_complex.go`,
  `validate/assess.go`. This entry rides the landing. **#1115 and #441 both
  close.**
- **Ratchet: unchanged.** The arbiter's figures, copied not recomputed:
  `GOXSD_RATCHET=1 go test ./conformance -run TestConformance -count=1` → `ok`
  in **330.830s** on the accepted commit, then `git status --porcelain --
  conformance/testdata/expectations/` — **empty**, and the full
  `git status --porcelain` empty with it. Nothing to bank; no removals
  asserted and none claimed (`GOXSD_RATCHET_REMOVALS` unset, discovery
  unchanged at 41759 cases with 99 withheld, no `Vanished` and no `Regressed`
  case). Gate part 4 at the accepted commit **PASS (330.04s)** — 41759 cases
  across 6 lanes, `datatypes` 1173 / `schema` 15398 / `instance` 26361,
  `xpath`/`json`/`ber` 0 — **no `regressed case(s)` and no `case(s) improved,
  not yet banked`** in any lane, the only other per-lane lines being the
  standing decline censuses (**datatypes 8, schema 196 + 11 indeterminate,
  instance 15315 + 5**) that `censusNotScore` marks as not-the-score. Parts
  1–3 clean at both commits: build and vet OK, `go test ./...` with every
  package `ok` (`conformance` 393.468s at the accepted commit), `go tool lint`
  **0 issues.**, `go tool commentwrap ./...` clean (`-fix` run once after the
  repair edit produced no further reflow, so the committed diff is exactly the
  three lines). Committed lane scores, `go tool lanestatus`, **identical in
  all three lanes across both rounds**: **datatypes 1161 / 12 / 1173, schema
  13830 / 1568 / 15398, instance 11029 / 15332 / 26361**. `go tool surface
  -base origin/main` → **`surface: unchanged`** both rounds. `go tool gapaudit`
  (empty `[]` on stdin, census mode) → **65 markers, `xsd` 32, and
  `xsd/ownedtypefold.go` appears nowhere in the census or in group 1** —
  #1115's *"gapaudit stops reporting the site"* bullet satisfied by
  measurement, and the census moves 66 → 65 off #815's landing earlier today.
- Cost — **1 oracle, 2 mason (1 repair), 2 arbiter (1 reject).** No warden, no
  cartographer, no steward. **The oracle bought the design fork outright**:
  #1115's Acceptance offered (a) and (b) as two live candidates and the
  grounding closed (b) from the spec rather than on cost, which is why the
  root set is untouched and the issue needed no re-scope from the
  cartographer's pen. It also bought the landing's shape — the queue search is
  what surfaced #441, and #441's Acceptance is what supplied the rule ID and
  the placement, so a session that had grounded only #1115 would have decided
  both by argument instead of reading them off a filed issue. The **reject
  round bought one sentence**, and the sentence is a real defect in an exported
  method's doc; what it cost is a second full four-part gate.
- Decisions made and why:
  - **`a-props-correct` clause 1, not `xsderr.RuleComponentInvariant`.**
    §3.2.1's tableau types `{type definition}` as a Simple Type Definition
    with no complex arm, and clause 1 is *"the values of the properties … are
    as described in the property tableau"* — so a wrapped `ComplexType` breaks
    a spec clause a CALLER violates, not a representation invariant this
    package owns (STYLE E2). Every other `ruleAPropsCorrect` site in the file
    stands on the same sentence about a different property.
  - **The check lives in `NewAttributeDeclaration`, not behind
    `checkTypeDefinitionOrRef`.** That checker's slot switch decides which ARM
    a slot admits, on this package's representation footing, and charges
    `RuleComponentInvariant` throughout; routing a spec verdict through it
    would make one switch return two rule families. This mirrors
    `NewElementDeclaration`'s existing `ownedComplexType()` rejection, which
    is the pattern #441 prescribed and which is still the live one.
  - **Ordering inside the constructor is deliberate**: `checkTypeDefinitionOrRef`
    runs FIRST, so a wrapped *named* `ComplexType` keeps its `component-invariant`
    charge (arm well-formedness before tableau conformance) and the new guard
    only ever fires on an otherwise well-formed anonymous inline type. Nothing
    pins that combination — the arbiter checked it from the source and ruled it
    **not a finding**, because the behaviour for that input is byte-identical
    to `origin/main`.
  - **The `Direction (STYLE P3a)` paragraph was deleted whole, not preserved.**
    P3a governs a claimed error direction on a GAP; with the gap gone there is
    no direction to substantiate. Its inventory of `ResolvedSimpleType`
    narrowing sites now exists nowhere in the tree — mason flagged exactly that
    as the thing to scrutinise hardest, and the arbiter ruled the loss real but
    not a cost: every site it named is one `grep -rn ResolvedSimpleType` away,
    and the invariant it argued for is enforced by construction now.
  - **Two cross-reference rewrites absorbed** (`parser/produce_complex.go`'s
    `produceComplexType`, `validate/assess.go`'s `walk.unmatchedAttribute`),
    one sentence each, no rule ID outside the grounding, no surface, no lane
    exposure — inside WORKFLOW's scope rule and named in the commit body
    rather than filed. `validate/assess.go`'s is the more consequential: it
    re-foots #1116's non-reachability assertion on the ENFORCEMENT instead of
    on a convention, which is verbatim the *"mechanical watch"* #1115's own
    Acceptance said option (a) would supply for that comment.
  - **`docs/PLAN.md:309` left alone and named rather than silently skipped.**
    Status row 6 still describes #1115's pre-change state. The arbiter ruled
    it out of scope on three grounds, and the first is general: CLAUDE.md's
    gate block is code-only, so there is no gate step a stale planning row
    could fail; the Status band is rewritten **by replacement** each
    `/backlog`, so hand-patching one row writes a fact into a table its owner
    will overwrite; and row 6 is a ranking rationale that leaves the band
    entirely once #1115 closes rather than being corrected.
- Surprises:
  - **The duplicate was a month old, better specified than the issue that
    found it, and neither thread had ever named the other.** #441 was filed
    2026-08-02 and #1115 did not exist yet; #1115 was filed later and never
    cross-referenced back. Both sat `ready` in the same queue, and the priority
    band ranked #1115 at row 6 while #441 was not in the band at all. Nothing
    in the queue's shape would have brought them together — the oracle's
    grounding-time queue search did, one step before implementation.
  - **A `go doc`-visible behaviour change at an exported constructor that
    `go tool surface` cannot see.** `NewAttributeDeclaration` starts returning
    an error for an input it accepted, and the surface diff is legitimately
    `unchanged` — no signature moved. Recorded below as a watch-item rather
    than a finding.
  - **The rejected shape is unreachable from everything except a hand-built
    `SchemaBuilder` caller, and the arbiter proved it in the parser rather
    than taking it asserted**: both `NewAttributeDeclaration` call sites fill
    the slot from `p.declaredType`, whose only inline arm is a `<simpleType>`
    child, and a `<complexType>` under `<attribute>` is a grammar fault at
    `checkS4SChildOrder` before that. So `Ratchet: unchanged` is the only
    figure the tree can produce, and no W3C-suite fixture can exercise the new
    rejection — which is why the test is a constructor-level one and not a
    conformance case.
- Friction — **the stale-marker sweep was incomplete in the same package as
  the deletion, and the tool that exists for markers cannot see the direction
  that failed.** `88ebe56`'s body asserts the sweep is complete and names two
  sites; the third, `xsd/complextype.go:1012`, is inside an EXPORTED method's
  doc, four hundred lines from the deletion, and the arbiter found it on a
  plain `grep -rn ownedtypefold`. `go tool gapaudit` reconciles markers
  against trackers — marker → issue — and nothing in the tree audits the other

**ZERO unblocked and the zero is MEASURED — no open issue was ever queued
behind either number and both were filed `Depends on: none`; TWO bodies
corrected where this landing falsified a premise, and in #584's case the
falsified premise was a MAPPING RULE the body listed as one of three producing
an anonymous complex type, deleted rather than struck through because a
struck-through mapping rule still reads as a shape to walk; TWO issues FILED
from the landing's own residue — #1167 for the second sighting the landing
entry recorded and routed nowhere, and #1168 for the watch-item whose reversal
trigger is detectable only by the failure it exists to prevent; ONE coincidental
same-day mention ruled UNRELATED with the mechanism named.** `docs/PLAN.md`
untouched, deliberately, and the reason is not the arbiter's ruling alone —
the row that ruling was about no longer exists.

- **Unblock sweep: ZERO, measured, across BOTH numbers.** Feed built with
  docs/ROUTINES.md's **Survey input** recipe against
  `repos/kud360/goxsd8/issues?state=open`, three pages, **251 open non-PR
  issues** at `8e89122` — one fewer than the `/backlog` pass's 252, #1115's and
  #441's closures being the difference against a feed that also gained #1164.
  **No open issue's `## Depends on` names #1115 or #441**, and none of the open
  `blocked` issues mentions either in one. Exactly **three** open bodies contain
  the strings at all: **#584** and **#438**, both corrected below, and **#1148**,
  where `#1115` appears only in the filing-time overlap list its Notes record
  (*"Searched the open queue before filing … **#1115**, **#1102**, **#1084** …
  None owns this wording"*) — a provenance record of a search that happened, not
  a premise that can go stale, so **#1148 is untouched and that is a
  disposition, not an omission**. Written down because a measured zero and a
  skipped sweep are otherwise indistinguishable from `git log` (#400).
- **`origin/wip/issue-1115` SURVIVES the merge**, unlike `wip/issue-815`, which
  was gone by its own post-land pass. Report-only, per the standing rule that
  sessions never delete or rename refs: its issue is CLOSED, its content is in
  `main` (`8e89122` is the squash of both its commits, and
  `git log origin/main` carries the tree), so it is a ref for human triage and
  not a lost landing. `wipsurvey` will read it RETIRED on the next `/backlog`.
- **#584 — the attribute-declaration bullet is DISCHARGED, and it was verified
  against the landed code rather than accepted from #1115's body.** #1115's own
  session had already corrected #584 to say the discharge *would* be by
  unrepresentability if option (a) landed; option (a) landed, so the conditional
  had to be collapsed and the tree had to be the thing that collapsed it. What
  was checked, at `8e89122`:
  - `xsd/attributedeclaration.go:428` rejects `ownedComplexType(typeDefinition)`
    and charges `ruleAPropsCorrect`, after `checkTypeDefinitionOrRef`.
  - **It is the only site in `xsd` that populates an `AttributeDeclaration`.**
    `grep -rn "AttributeDeclaration{" --include=*.go xsd/` outside tests returns
    one construction — `attributedeclaration.go:440`, inside that constructor —
    and every other hit is a `AttributeDeclaration{}` zero-value return. All
    eight struct fields are unexported, so a struct literal is not a bypass
    either. That is what makes the discharge *unrepresentability* rather than
    *unproduced*, and it is the half a reader cannot take from the commit body,
    which argues only that no parser and no validate consumer reaches the shape.
  - The three slots #584's Direction named all reach that one constructor:
    a top-level attribute declaration's `{type definition}`,
    `LocalAttributeDeclaration` (`xsd/attributeuse.go:66` — a struct wrapping an
    `AttributeDeclaration` VALUE, so it inherits the chokepoint), and one inside
    an attribute group definition. Both parser call sites go through it
    (`parser/produce.go:2828`, `parser/produce_complex.go:2902`).
  - `TestInlineTypeDefinitionVarietyBySlot` pins the rejection and drives
    `NewElementDeclaration` off the same table, so the element-side contrast is
    pinned in one place rather than asserted in two.

## 2026-09-01 — #438 — **cos-nonambig, cos-element-consistent and the three §3.4.6 constraints charge an anonymous complex type a declaration owns for the first time — `complexTypeWalk` (`xsd/complextypewalk.go`) is one document-order descent from `s.types`, `s.elements`' three `ownedTypeSlots` and `s.modelGroups`, the three read-only finalize passes take it in place of their `s.types` loops, and the `schema` lane banks `13830 → 13858` pass / `1568 → 1540` fail on 28 new rejections with zero regressed and zero vanished**: the exposure was latent until #1126 deleted `anonymousComplexTypeDecidable` four days ago and put every previously-withheld document in front of both executors, so this landing closes a LIVE under-rejection rather than a theoretical one; the **oracle ruled the issue's own first Acceptance bullet UNSATISFIABLE AS WORDED** — its root set (`s.types` plus each element declaration's singular `InlineTypeDefinition`) omits `s.modelGroups` as an independent root, since a top-level `<group name>` no `<group ref>` reachable from `s.types` names is reachable from nothing else, and omits the `{type table}` slots §3.3.2.1 makes co-occurring with the element's own inline type — and the **cartographer corrected the body before implementation started**, so the walk was built against a three-root set that was right on the first attempt; mason took **neither of the issue's two implementer options** but a fourth three-root walk that REUSES `ownedTypeSlots` rather than re-enumerating it, because a type-table capability on the shared `componentWalk` would give its two existing callers new e-props-correct and value-constraint verdicts this issue cannot attribute, and a `complexType` charge hook on it would charge the `<redefine>` original that is #584's; the arbiter **REJECTED** round 1 on three findings that are all statements-about-the-change rather than the change — two accuracy defects in prose (the new GAP markers enumerated the surviving residue as the base-seated redefine original ALONE, missing the anonymous type a local `<element>` owns inside a `<redefine>`d `<group>`'s original, and the walk's own head claimed `ownedTypeFold` descends from the same three roots when it descends from four, the omitted fourth being exactly that residue) and one **STYLE E1** that the landing itself made live — ~26 message sites in `complexderivation.go`/`complexextension.go` rendered a type by its bare `{name}`, which for a declaration-owned type is the zero QName, so three of the 28 cases this landing newly rejects printed *"complex type  extends base …"* with a hole where the reader looks for the construct; **repair round 1 delivered all three**, swapping 32 rendered subjects onto the existing `complexTypeOwner` and adding a third marker at the root set itself, and **round 2 ACCEPTED with zero findings** after the arbiter built `judge` binaries from `origin/main` and from `dca4f63`, ran both over all **15470** suite `.xsd` files and diffed **30k+ output lines**: 29 changed hunks, 28 `(accepted)` → rejection, **0 rejections → accepted and 0 message-text changes on any already-reported rejection**.

- Cost — **1 oracle, 1 cartographer, 2 mason (1 repair), 2 arbiter (1
  reject).** No warden (`surface: unchanged`), no steward. **The oracle bought
  the root set**, and it is the one purchase that changed the shipped code
  rather than its prose: the filed Acceptance would have produced a walk that
  misses an unreferenced `<group>`'s inline types and every
  `<alternative>`-borne one, and the correction landed in the body before
  mason read it, so no round was spent discovering it. **The reject round
  bought three prose-and-rendering fixes and one of them is a user-visible
  defect this landing created** — the E1 holes exist only on the types the
  widening newly reaches — at the price of a second full four-part gate. Four
  four-part gates plus a ratchet write run: part 4 at **327.2s, 319.1s,
  324.7s, 342.99s** and **333.2s** for the write, ~28 minutes of conformance,
  with `go test ./...`'s own `conformance` at 392.8s, 405.0s and 395.9s
  alongside.
- Decisions made and why:
  - **A fourth three-root walk in `xsd`, not a widened `componentWalk`.** The
    issue offered "extend `componentWalk` with a type-table capability, or
    hand-roll the descent"; both were declined for what the shared walk
    ENUMERATES, not what it charges. Entering `{type table}` slots in
    `walkElementDeclaration` would make `checkTypeTableSubstitutability` charge
    e-props-correct clause 7 against declarations nested inside
    alternative-owned types, and `checkComponentValueConstraints` clause 2 the
    same — new verdicts in passes this issue does not own and cannot attribute
    to its own ratchet movement. A `complexType` hook on `componentWalk` would
    charge the `<redefine>` original its `walkComplexType` deliberately enters
    as a component in its own right (#843), which is #584's change. What is
    NOT duplicated is the slot enumeration: `walkElementDeclaration` calls
    `ownedTypeSlots`, the single producer, whose doc now names three readers.
    **The cost is stated rather than hidden — `xsd` now holds four three-root
    walks** (`componentwalk.go` + its two callers, `ownedtypefold.go`,
    `allgrouplimited.go`'s hand-rolled one, and this) — and both mason and the
    arbiter ruled it a **T4 question for the steward, not a correctness one**,
    since `checkAllGroupsLimited` already covers the anonymous shape by its own
    descent.
  - **An owned `{base type definition}` is entered to reach the declarations
    inside it and charged nothing** — the same split
    `ownedTypeFold.ownedBase`/`.ownedTypeSlot` already draws — because the
    body's exclusion of `s.modelGroupRedefinitions` is an exclusion of the
    base-seating mechanism generally. Pinned by
    `TestWalkEntersAnOwnedBaseWithoutChargingIt`'s two subtests.
  - **No visited set, and the soundness argument is the walk's, not an
    inheritance.** Every edge followed is an ownership edge — `ResolvedTerm`
    wraps an already-known `Term` and `<element ref>`/`<group ref>` are never
    rewritten into it, `ownedComplexType` admits only the
    `InlineTypeDefinition` arm — so the structure is a finite tree and
    PRINCIPLES 9 holds without a guard. One anonymous type can still be
    charged more than once (a `{default type definition}` aliasing the
    declaration's own type, a base's particle inside an extension's); each
    pass decides a property of the component alone at a `Loc` the component
    carries, so this is verdict-preserving and costs **~4% of conformance wall
    time** (327s against a 313s pre-change baseline on the same machine).
  - **A THIRD GAP marker, at the root set, beyond the two the verdict named.**
    Mason's judgement call, flagged as one; the arbiter ruled it in scope
    under WORKFLOW's scope rule (no rule ID the grounding lacks, no exported
    surface, cannot move a lane) and kept it, because a gap whose cause is the
    root set belongs where the root set is chosen and the other two markers get
    shorter by pointing at it. **No new issue filed for the residue** — #584's
    title already scopes it as "an ANONYMOUS complex type reached only through
    an owning slot gets no Phase D verdict".
  - **`validate/icfixture_test.go`'s `icAnonymousSchema` was spec-invalid and
    is now an extension.** Its `<kid>` type was an anonymous RESTRICTION of a
    `Base` with no attribute use and no `{attribute wildcard}` while declaring
    `@aid` and `@ref` of its own — forbidden by derivation-ok-restriction
    clause 3 (c-ran), and finalizing only because no pass reached it. The
    arbiter checked the reading against `xmlschema11-1.md:2038` and
    `:1964` rather than taking it (an *E* carrying `@aid` is attributed by
    c-aam clause 2.1 against *T*, falls to clause 2.2 against *B*, and 2.2.1
    demands a wildcard `Base` does not have) and confirmed the switch is inert:
    §3.4.2.4 clause 3 unions in nothing from an empty base, so the
    `{attribute uses}` all three tests read are byte-identical.
- Surprises:
  - **The widening found a spec-invalid test fixture in another package.**
    Three `validate` tests failed on first build, and the failure was correct —
    a fixture that had never been legal, held up only by the absence of the
    verdict this issue adds. Mason named it the single thing it most wanted
    scrutinised, which is what got it checked against the spec rather than
    patched.
  - **A 29th hunk the ratchet cannot see.** The arbiter's binary diff caught
    `msData/simpleType/stZ012.xsd` changing which error is reported FIRST, from
    `st-props-correct` to `cos-nonambig` — both hold, and the case rejects

    reachable violation charged in an earlier phase is exactly what this
    landing is for, and **only a whole-suite output diff surfaces it**; the
    expectation file cannot.
  - **The body predicted `Ratchet: unchanged` and the movement is +28 upward.**
    Its Acceptance asked for "no behaviour change for any named type (verify:
    ratchet unchanged for any currently-passing case)"; both halves are true
    at once, and closing an under-rejection is supposed to produce exactly

  - **The two prose findings share one root: a quotation truncated at an
    em-dash.** The GROUNDING quoted `xsd/ownedtypefold.go:55-60`'s root-set
    sentence up to the dash, dropping "**plus the `<redefine>` ORIGINALS**";

## 2026-09-01 — #1129 — **`docs/WORKFLOW.md`'s Landing section states, for the first time, what a PR that closes NO issue must verify and who verifies it — one paragraph after the existing four preconditions, transferring the verifier duty to the agent that opens the PR rather than redefining "orchestrating session", and keeping precondition 4's read-back whole rather than narrowing it to keyword-closed references**: the four preconditions were written only against the `wip/issue-<N>` code-landing shape, so for the cartographer's own `post-land` PR (required to exist by #1109, `48310e8`) or a `/backlog` PR, preconditions 3 and 4 were unsatisfiable by construction — no mason round and no owning issue thread for 3, and a body that deliberately names issues without closing them for 4 — while the verifier clause named *"the orchestrating session"* for a PR the cartographer actually opens and merges; the arbiter **REJECTED** round 1 on two findings that are both *the paragraph making claims it had no business making*, not errors about the shape — an early precondition-4 clause narrowing the binding set to *"the issues the squash body closes with a keyword"*, which is verbatim what precondition 4 rules out (*"bound closing keyword and plain mention alike"*) and which, inside this shape, scopes the check to the empty set and so verifies nothing, with the discarded net demonstrated LIVE in the branch's own base — `7315987`'s squash body carries <code>the overturned "#584's landing closes #438" ruling</code>, a binding keyword inside a quotation — and an early verifier clause saying *"that agent is the orchestrating session for this shape"*, which makes WORKFLOW contradict CLAUDE.md's cast definition (coordinates, does no specialist work, never skips the arbiter) of the very term `:256-258` is written in; **one repair round delivered both** by stating the expected reading instead of narrowing the scope (*"Precondition 4 runs its full procedure here, every `#<N>` the squash body names read back alike … so `open` is an expected reading"*) and by moving the duty instead of the label (*"verifies and states these preconditions in the orchestrating session's place"*, reusing `:256-258`'s own verb pair so a grep for either lands on both sites); **round 2 ACCEPTED with zero findings**, `Ratchet: unchanged` run rather than inferred at both rounds, `surface: unchanged` at both, **1 file, +14/−0**, a pure insertion with **zero deletions and zero modified lines**, so *"the four preconditions are not weakened for the code-landing shape"* is proved mechanically rather than asserted.

- Shipped on `wip/issue-1129`, base `origin/main` at **`7315987`**, unmoved
  through grounding, both implementation rounds and both verdicts
  (`git log HEAD..origin/main` empty at each) — no merge forward, no
  re-judgment on a moved base. Two commits: **`13ec161`** (`meta: state the
  landing contract for a PR that closes no issue (#1129)`, rejected, 1 file,
  +12/−0) and **`144119b`** (`meta: keep precondition 4's read-back and
  transfer the verifier duty (#1129)`, the repair, 1 file, +13/−11 on top;
  nothing amended, nothing force-pushed). Branch total from `7315987`:
  **1 file, +14/−0** — `docs/WORKFLOW.md` only, one hunk
  `@@ -298,6 +298,20 @@`, the new paragraph at `:301-313` between
  precondition 4 and `### After the verdict`. The repair's +13/−11 is
  **wrap, not prose**: the arbiter's `git diff --word-diff=plain 13ec161
  144119b` shows every changed span inside the two flagged sentences and
  **zero word changes** in the precondition-1, -2 and -3 clauses. This entry
  rides the landing. **#1129 closes.**

  **both** rounds — *"ratchet run, tree clean, nothing to bank"* each time,

  that verification; a doc-only diff touching no `.go` file had nothing to

  473.577s`, `144119b` `ok 408.838s` — **41759 cases across 6 lanes, 99

  `FAIL`, `go tool lint` **0 issues**, `go tool commentwrap ./...` clean with

  - **Precondition 4 keeps its full procedure for this shape; only the
    expected READING is stated.** The rejected draft narrowed what binds and
    then supplied the outcome (*"those read `open` correctly"*) in place of
    the per-issue read-back — which inside this shape iterates the empty set.
    The repair says the procedure runs and that `open` is *"an expected
    reading"*: the indefinite article is load-bearing, because an `open` the

    quoted-keyword hazard.

    orchestrating session's place"* leaves CLAUDE.md's cast definition true of
    exactly who it was true of before, and reconciles rather than contradicts
    `:256-258`'s *"None is anyone else's to volunteer"* — an explicit

  - **"Precondition 3 does not bind" stays flat rather than conditional.** A
    PR that closes no issue has no owning issue thread by construction, so the
    check has no referent; the narrower *"does not bind when there is no mason
    round"* is a conditional this tree has no instance of. The paragraph also
    says where the account DOES go — the LOG entry and the PR body — so the
    flat form cannot be read as *no account is owed*.
  - **Precondition 1 is cited in five words and never duplicated.** Its
    existing carve-out already covers this shape; `post-land` appears once in
    the new paragraph, carrying the verifier fact, and `/backlog` does not
    reappear.
- Surprises:
  - **The check the rejected draft retired had a live instance in the branch's
    own base.** `7315987` — the commit this branch is based on, a `post-land`
    PR, the exact shape the paragraph is about — carries `closes #438` inside

## 2026-09-01 — post-land pass for #1129

**ZERO unblocked and the zero is measured; ONE issue filed — #1174, and it is not
the issue the landing entry pointed at; THREE follow-ups dismissed, each with the
trigger that reverses it; TWO threads ruled and one CLOSED thread annotated.**
`docs/PLAN.md` untouched, deliberately. **The pass's finding is that the landing
entry's Friction item rested on a false premise**: the gate-cost question it
recorded as *"already a queued `/retro` question raised by #1145's landing"* was
ruled and CLOSED as decided eight days before #1145 landed, as **#881**, and five
entries dated today second the re-raise without naming it.

- **Unblock sweep: ZERO, measured.** **No open issue's `## Depends on` names
  #1129**, and none of the **20** open `blocked` issues mentions it in one. Feed
  taken after the merge from the REST issues endpoint per `docs/ROUTINES.md`'s
  **Survey input** recipe — 12 pages at `per_page=100`, page 12 short at 73 and
  **re-requested once** per #1145's landed distrust rule (73 both times, page 13
  empty), **668** non-PR issues, **252** open, **232** `ready`, **20** `blocked`.
  Exactly one open body contains the digits `1129` and it is **not a reference**:
  #558 cites `conformance/datatypes.go:1129` in a call-site list. #1129 was filed
  `Depends on: none` and nothing was ever queued behind it. Written down because a
  measured zero and a skipped sweep are otherwise indistinguishable from
  `git log` (#400). `wip/issue-1129` is gone from `origin`, as a merged lease
  should be.
- **The Friction item was CHECKED before it was dismissed, and the check is this
  pass's whole finding.** The landing entry priced three four-part gates plus two
  ratchet write-runs on a diff with zero executable lines and routed it as
  *"already a queued `/retro` question … so this session is its **fourth** data
  point and not a new finding."* It is not queued. **#881** —
  *"process: the gate has no doc-only arm"* — asked the identical question, and
  the seventh weekly `/retro` answered it on **2026-08-23**: **keep the gate
  uniform**, `issuecomment-5386394011`, also at `docs/LOG/2026-08.md:42347-42357`.
  Part 4 IS the ratchet check and conditioning it is reserved to a human-filed
  issue; #735's `commentonly` detector does not exist, so a gate conditioned today
  would be conditioned on nothing. **So nothing about gate cost is filed by this
  pass and nothing is queued** — the ruling stands and reopening it is a human's,
  after #735 lands.
- **What IS untracked is the re-raise, and it is measured rather than asserted.**
  #881's own Acceptance predicted this failure in its own words — *"the cost is

filed about — title and labels only, body untouched; TWO issues filed — #1177 for

and now stale in four places named below. **The pass's own finding is #1178, and
it was produced by running the landing entry's `Next:` item 3 rather than by
looking for anything**: `2355fe0`'s body opens `Closed #625, #748, #492, #934,
#896 …` — a closing keyword heading a comma list, the form `docs/WORKFLOW.md`
forbids by name — and eleven lines later certifies that those five are *"named
here as plain mentions, not closing keywords."* The rule it breaks was landed by
**#1034** on 2026-08-26, and this is the next landing whose entire subject is
closing-keyword semantics.

  issue's `## Depends on` names #1023, #625, #748, #492, #934 or #896**, and

  `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/issues/1174`, which `docs/ROUTINES.md:86-89` ranks
  as the byte-faithful channel — the MCP path escapes and deletes exactly the

  characters and ended with **72** that were never part of it:
  `…, naming neither</title>` newline `<parameter name="labels">["ready",
  "kind/process", "area/meta"]`, and `labels` was **`[]`**. So the filing call's

  as *"**#1174 FILED — `ready`, `kind/process`, `area/meta`**"* — **the LOG and

    Title cut at the `</title>` marker — mechanically, not by retyping — leaving
    the real text whole at **255** characters ending *"…, naming neither"*;
    nothing authored was lost and the 72 discarded characters were the corruption
    in full. Labels applied as **`ready`, `kind/process`, `area/meta`**: the three
    the LOG records, cross-checked against the body's own terms rather than
