## 2026-09-02 — #472 — **`goxsd8 parse` is the first subcommand that does work, and the whole summary is built from #252's published accessors and nothing else (`surface: unchanged` at both rounds, which is exactly what sequencing this after #252 bought)**: `parse <schema.xsd>...` compiles each argument and prints its namespaces, a count per §3.17.1 property and a total on stdout, exit 0 when every argument compiles, **1** with the first error on stderr in `(*xsderr.Error).Error()`'s own rendering, and **2** for a usage or IO fault — the split made by **opening the document before `parser.Parse` sees it** rather than by archaeology on the returned error chain, which is what keeps `is a directory` (charged `[xml-wf]` by the parser) out of the verdict bucket; the six decisions the body demanded are stated once in the three copies that must agree (`doc.go`, `main.go`'s `usage` const, `README.md`) — one run per argument in argument order with the exit code the worst outcome, **git-style** positional grammar with a fourth diagnosis so `goxsd8 -q parse a.xsd` stops answering *"no subcommand given"* while every pinned `dispatchCases` row survives, `-q` scoped to informational output and **never** a diagnosis (the rule that binds `validate`'s stdout violation lines from #1066 too), `-help=true` a usage error at every position, and `-version` kept a non-flag; the **`Spec:` line is `n/a` on the oracle's finding, not by omission** — `xmlschema11-1.md` §2.4's own note disclaims the subject outright (*"This specification defines no API or other interface for interacting with schemas"*), so the only spec contact is that the CLI is a **pure conduit** for whatever rule ID the parser already attached; the arbiter **REJECTED** round 1 on eight findings of which **not one was about a design decision** — two of the three tests mason's own account named load-bearing **could not fail**, and the arbiter proved both by mutation rather than by reading, because no fixture had two namespaces (so `namespacesOf`, the diff's only order-producing code, was unpinned and the map-range mutant its own comment names passed the whole package) and the single error fixture returned **unwrapped** (so deleting `violationLine`'s `errors.As` unwrap shipped green, with `parser:` in the contract's location field); *"one line per error on stderr"* was meanwhile **false of shipped behaviour in five places**, `parser/parse.go:146` returning only the first error; the repair delivered all eight, bought a two-namespace `<xs:import>` pair **named so that first-appearance and sorted order disagree** and a malformed-XML document the parser returns wrapped, and filed **#1185** so the `GAP(cmd)` marker earns a tracker in the order `docs/WORKFLOW.md:449` runs it; round 2 **ACCEPTED with no findings** after the arbiter re-ran all four mutations itself — two of them its own — against a 55-run clean baseline; **`Ratchet: unchanged`, measured not assumed** (`go list -deps ./conformance | grep -c cmd/goxsd8` is `0`, so no lane can observe this diff), 11 files **+1032/−91**, nothing exported.

- Shipped on `wip/issue-472`, base `origin/main` at **`a7fa3ae`**, unmoved through
  grounding, both implementation rounds and both verdicts. Two commits, nothing
  amended and nothing force-pushed, so the reject stays legible in history:
  **`20c5594`** (`cmd: implement `goxsd8 parse`, the first subcommand that does
  real work (#472)` — `parse.go`, `parse_test.go`, the `order.xsd`/`broken.xsd`
  fixtures and the `main.go`/`doc.go`/`main_test.go`/`README.md` edits; REJECTED)
  and **`179ae53`** (`cmd: address arbiter repair round 1 on #472 …` — 10 files,
  +157/−36). Branch total from `a7fa3ae`: **11 files, +1032/−91** —
  `README.md`, `cmd/goxsd8/{doc.go,main.go,main_test.go,parse.go,parse_test.go}`
  and five fixtures under `cmd/goxsd8/testdata/`. This entry rides the landing.
  **#472 closes.** It is `docs/PLAN.md` band row **5**, *"the only `ready`
  `kind/feature` on M4, and the single ref that gates the CLI's entire remaining
  queue"*; **#720 (`goxsd8 validate`) is what it unblocks**, and #16 waits on #720.
- **Ratchet: unchanged.** The arbiter's figures, copied not recomputed, and
  structural rather than asserted: `cmd/goxsd8` is absent from
  `go list -deps ./conformance`, so `go list -deps ./conformance | grep -c
  cmd/goxsd8` is `0` and no lane can observe a `cmd` diff. `GOXSD_RATCHET=1 go
  test ./conformance` ran at both rounds and left
  `git status --porcelain -- conformance/testdata/expectations/` **EMPTY**, checked
  immediately after the run. Gate all four parts green at both judged commits —
  part 1 exit 0 with no `FAIL` line in any package (`conformance` 520.805s at
  `20c5594`, 520.917s at `179ae53`), `go tool lint` **0 issues.**, `commentwrap`
  clean with no reflow, part 4 **PASS** (438.697s, then 414.755s) with **no
  improved-but-unbanked line on any lane**. `go tool surface -base origin/main`
  → **`surface: unchanged`** at both rounds (**T5**), which is structural too:
  `cmd/goxsd8` is `package main` and exports nothing, so no warden pass was owed.
  `git submodule update --init testdata/xsdtests` ran first at both rounds (#659);
  the 41759-case discovery line confirms the suite was populated.
- Cost — **1 oracle, 2 mason (1 repair), 2 arbiter (1 reject).** No warden
  (`surface: unchanged`, confirmed by outcome), no cartographer (**mason filed
  #1185 itself**, as the marker's tracker rather than as planning), no steward.
  **What the reject bought is two dead tests made live and one false contract
  clause retired — not a different answer about the change**: round 1 accepted
  every design decision on the merits, including the one mason asked to have
  scrutinized hardest, and still found that the diff's own account of which tests
  were load-bearing was wrong about two of three.
- Decisions made and why:
  - **Multi-schema is one run per argument, not one combined set.** `parser.Parse`
    takes a single root location, so a combined set needs a library change and is
    out of scope; the runs are independent, errors are **not** deduped, every
    argument is reported however the one before it went, and the exit code is the
    max over 0/1/2 with the constants ordered by severity for that purpose. Beats
    stop-at-first-failure, which hides verdicts one pass could have given.
  - **The exit-2 split is made at the seam, not by classifying an error.** Opening
    the document in `rootLocation` before handing it to `parser.Parse` is what
    produces `goxsd8: parse: open testdata/nosuch.xsd: no such file or directory`
    instead of a three-clause loader chain, and it is what makes
    `TestParseFaultsAreDistinguishable` a property test rather than a shape
    assertion: five kinds, distinct codes, pairwise-distinct stderr. This
    discharges the asymmetry the thread has carried since 2026-08-16 — `validate`'s
    blurb reserved 2 and `parse`'s did not.
  - **Git-style grammar, with the fourth diagnosis rather than a second parse of
    the vocabulary.** `leadingFlagFmt` fires only when a real subcommand follows;
    `noSubcommand` survives for a leading flag with nothing after it, so the
    `-version` and `-help=true` rows stay pinned. Go-style — a leading flag as a
    usage error naming the missing subcommand — would have meant a second
    pre-dispatch parse of the same flag names, which is a second encoding of them
    (D3). Two independent cliuser witnesses had reported the old misdiagnosis, and
    the `/backlog` declined to split it out precisely because the grammar cannot be
    stated until a subcommand parses its own flags; this is that subcommand.
  - **The resolver is rooted at the filesystem root, dropping `loader.Dir`'s
    confinement — accepted on the merits, and the repair added the disclosure.**
    It is the only rooting under which all three hold at once: an absolute or `../`
    argument works, a relative `<xs:include>` resolves against its own document's
    directory, and the `<loc>` an error cites is a path the reader can open (the
    conformance harness's base-name pattern would print `broken.xsd:9:3` for
    `parse a/b/broken.xsd` and fail that bar). `loader/dir.go:19-22` frames the
    confinement as a defence against a location *"treated as an untrusted, possibly
    attacker-supplied hint"* and calls it an engineering decision, not a spec rule;
    a CLI reading documents its own invoking user named, with that user's
    privileges, has no attacker in that position. **The finding was never the
    behaviour — it was that a changed security-relevant default lived only in a
    source comment**, so `doc.go`'s Argument vocabulary now says *"confined to no
    subtree: a schema document may name any path the invoking user can read, so an
    `<xs:include schemaLocation="../../../etc/passwd">` is served like any other"*,
    and the arbiter ruled the concrete consequence better than the sentence it had
    asked for.
  - **The summary reports the namespaces the compiled set's components are in, and
    that is an API finding recorded rather than an export.** `*xsd.Schema` has no
    target-namespace accessor and **§3.17.1 gives the Schema component no `{target
    namespace}` property at all** — it belongs to each schema *document*. The
    residual is honest and stays open: a document with `targetNamespace="X"` that
    declares no global components contributes no namespace line, because nothing
    observable carries X. Reporting a document's declared namespace needs a new
    accessor and a warden pre-flight; nothing was exported.
  - **Each bucket is filtered to components carrying a source position.** `xsd`'s
    package doc is what fixes the zero `Loc` as *"the correct value for a component
    with no schema document behind it"*; unfiltered, every schema reports the same
    54 seeded builtins instead of `order.xsd`'s 2. Flagged by mason for scrutiny
    and ruled sound.
  - **The vacuous `parser:` assertion was MOVED, not duplicated.** The
    `src-resolve` fixture returns unwrapped and can never reach the unwrap branch,
    so leaving a check there that no mutation can fail is the defect the reject
    named. The arbiter confirmed the move cost nothing: under the unwrap mutation
    the full-package run names exactly one `--- FAIL`.
  - **The error-count clause is amended, not defended.** The issue's own Spec
    section granted *"implement it as written, or amend all three copies
    deliberately and in one commit"*; `parser/parse.go:146` is the authority
    (*"Like Produce, ParseReport returns only the first error"*), and the amended
    clause landed in `doc.go`, `usage`, `README.md` and the two further sites the
    verdict had not named — `main_test.go`'s pins and `parse_test.go`'s own doc
    comment. `README.md`'s *one per line* for `validate` is left alone: that is
    `validate`'s unbuilt contract, not a claim about shipped behaviour.
- Surprises:
  - **The reject was entirely about test quality, and the tests it killed are the
    ones mason's own account nominated.** The account's *"the ones that would catch
    a mutation rather than decorate the diff"* list had three entries and two of
    them could not fail. Nothing in the diff's design was wrong; the arbiter said
    so at length and rejected anyway.
  - **A `-count=1` package run is not a mutation test.** The map-range mutant
    survived thirteen tests and a 21-run byte comparison because the fixture had
    one namespace, so `order` never held two elements and map iteration had nothing
    to permute — what the determinism test proved was that the *counts* were
    stable, which no map was ever going to threaten.
  - **Go 1.26's iteration over the repaired two-entry map is not an even coin, and
    the arbiter stated the residual rather than rounding it to "fixed"**:
    `TestParseNamespaceOrder` alone catches the mutant ~15% of the time and the
    21-run comparison ~95%, which is 21 draws at p≈0.135, so roughly one run in
    thirty lets it through the package. **Not a finding** — the committed code is
    deterministic across 55 clean runs and the 21 is the count round 1 itself
    prescribed — but the knob is recorded: the loop count *is* the sensitivity,
    and 20 → 100 takes the escape to ~1e-6.
  - **The fixture pair was named so first-appearance and sorted order disagree**,
    which is why two different mutation classes are covered by two tests that are
    not redundant: `slices.Sort` fails the golden block 3/3, the map range fails
    the determinism loop. The arbiter verified both claims rather than reading them.
  - **`helpCases`' new `parse` row was worth having for a reason neither agent
    predicted.** `wantsHelp` runs before dispatch, so `parse -h` was never at risk
    today; the row's value is that it now stops a future reordering from routing
    `-h` into `runParse`'s `flag.FlagSet`, which answers it `helpNotAFlagValue` and
    exit 2 — the arbiter proved that by mutating the dispatch order and watching
    both drivers fail.
  - **The one-line-per-error phrase was true as contract prose and became false the
    moment `parse` shipped.** Nobody wrote it wrongly; T6 turned three copies of an
    aspiration into three claims about behaviour, and the landing that implements a
    contract is the landing that has to re-read every sentence of it.
- Friction — **one pattern, and it is now two sessions deep.** Both of the last
  two code landings — #786 earlier today, then this one — were rejected on a
  **`[tests that cannot fail]`** finding against a test the mason account had
  itself nominated as load-bearing: #786's guard fixture
  repaired with a witness carrying the identical defect, and this session's
  determinism and unwrap tests. Twice the arbiter found it by running a mutation
  that mason had described in a comment and not executed. Routed to `/retro` below;
  the entry point is the mason account, not the verdict. Everything else this
  session paid is ruled or documented, one clause each: the submodule init per
  fresh worktree (#659); the full four-part gate plus a ratchet write-run at every
  round, ruled by the seventh `/retro` as **#881 — keep the gate uniform** — at
  414–521s per part-1 run here; and `docs/PLAN.md` untouched with band row 5 now
  stale, which step 6 retires by replacing the Status section **whole**, exactly as
  #1120's, #1129's, #1023's, #1115's and #786's passes ruled.

  disclosure is load-bearing rather than incidental.** Reproduced against the
  built binary rather than reasoned about: a document declaring
  `targetNamespace="http://example.com/empty"` and no global components prints its
  location, **no namespace line**, seven zero counts and `components: 0`, exit 0.
  The contract does not over-promise it — all three copies say the summary prints
  *"the namespaces its components are in"*, which is literally what it does, and
  seven visible zeroes make the absent line self-explanatory rather than silent —
  and `summarize`'s doc comment states the mechanism with the citation: §3.17.1
  gives the Schema component no `{target namespace}` property, so a document's
  `targetNamespace` is observable only through the components it declares. **The
  filing condition is a judgment nobody has made**: reporting the document's own
  `targetNamespace` needs a new accessor on `*xsd.Schema` or
  `parser.AssemblyReport`, which is a warden question about library surface and
  not a `parse` bug. Filing it now would put a `kind/gap` in the queue whose
  Acceptance is *"decide whether we want this"*. The reproduction is written here
  so a later judgment starts from a measurement.

## 2026-09-02 — #1160 — **all TWELVE decided-and-wrong `instance` cases carry an owner for the first time, and every one of the four #1120's verdict left unaccounted went to an issue that already existed — `gMonth002_2061.v` and `gMonth004_2063.v` to #921, `open013.v1.xml` to #456, and `SType/st_targetns00101m/instance/ST_targetNS00101m2_p` to #771 as its SECOND case — so `docs/PLAN.md` states a settled 9/3 partition where it carried a *"do not act on the count"* caveat**: the twelve were re-derived live at HEAD `c720206` from the instruments that print them rather than quoted from #1120's thread — `go tool lanestatus` reading `instance | 11029 pass | 15332 fail | 26361 total` against `GOXSD_DECLINES=1`'s `15315 declined case(s) recorded fail` and `5 further case(s) declined as indeterminate` — and mason and the arbiter each did the **set subtraction** rather than the bare arithmetic (`candidates − fails` empty, residual **17** = 12 + 5), which is #1120's own *"take a figure from the instrument that produces it"* rule applied to the very figure it was landed for; the one non-trivial ruling — `m2_p` → #771 — was REPRODUCED from the suite and not inferred, `SType.testSet:3045` carrying one `schemaDocument` with zero `import`/`include`/`redefine` and `ST_targetNS00101ma.xsd` referenced from nowhere but the instances' own `xsi:schemaLocation`, so the root `{ST_targetNSa}test` and its rescuing `xsi:type` resolve into the same unassembled namespace; the question #1120's post-land pass filed as a labelled HYPOTHESIS — whether an unresolvable `xsi:type` is a distinct arm from #771's undeclared-root arm, and so whether the case needs a fifth issue — is settled **NO from the code**, `validate/cvcelt.go`'s `instanceTypeDefinition` returning `(nil, false)` for absent, non-QName and unresolved-QName alike in one conjunction (its doc comment citing W3C issue 11764), so both routes charge `cvc-assess-elt` and **#771 needed a thread comment, not a body edit or a new issue**; the arbiter **REJECTED** round 1 on a defect the attribution itself created — the class paragraph's *"the first is a single case — #771"* left false by the new paragraph four lines above it and reconciled with a caveat clause rather than in the sentence making the claim, which is the same move as the text this branch deletes, one level down and one case narrower — and the repair re-split the block **9/3**, moved `m2_p` into the class that owns it so the case ID appears exactly once, and restated class 1 as **one root cause, two routes**, the root-name lookup failing in the `ElemDecl` case and the `xsi:type` lookup in the `SType` one; round 2 **ACCEPTED with zero blocking findings and two non-blocking notes**, `Ratchet: unchanged` run rather than inferred, `surface: unchanged`, **1 file `+23/−13`**, no `.go` file, no `conformance/testdata/expectations/` file and no executable line anywhere in the branch.

  - **#1188 came in framed as a bug and left as a surface question, which is a
    bigger issue and not a smaller one.** cliuser reported the `parse` summary
    omitting its namespace line for a schema that declares a `targetNamespace` and
    no global components, while a schema with no `targetNamespace` prints
    `namespace: (absent)`. The tree says the omission is **structural**: §3.17.1
    gives the Schema component no `{target namespace}` property, so
    `cmd/goxsd8/parse.go`'s `namespacesOf` derives the list from component names
    **by necessity**, and there is nothing on `*xsd.Schema` to read instead. Filed
    as a **warden question with two branches** — expose the namespace as new `xsd`
    surface, or document the derivation — **neither picked**, because picking one
    is the warden's call and not a cartographer's. This is the same residual #472's
    post-land pass **declined to file** on the ground that its filing condition was
    a judgment nobody had made; an outsider hitting it independently is that
    condition arriving.
  - **#1189 is a straight contradiction between shipped behaviour and shipped
    documentation.** `goxsd8 -help=true` before the subcommand answers `goxsd8: no
    subcommand given` instead of naming the three help spellings, which is what
    `cmd/goxsd8/doc.go:61-64` says happens *"wherever they stand"*.

## 2026-09-02 — #1164 — **the 584/588 near-match is ruled a COINCIDENCE and `docs/PLAN.md` says so before any stamp can cite it as a prediction: the chain from bucket to lane is `584 discoveries → 580 documents → 475 movers → 588 lines`, whose three factors — 0.993 documents per discovery, a yield of 0.819 movers per bucket document, a fan-out of 1.238 lines per mover — multiply to 1.007, so the agreement is an 18% shortfall and a 24% fan-out cancelling and NONE of the three is statable before a landing**: the 584 was **re-derived at `adb6d57`, the commit it was measured on**, rather than quoted — `git archive` to a scratch tree, `anonymousComplexTypeDecidable`'s two arms split to record a decline reason, `TestUnmappedCensusSoundAgainstShapeGate`'s own loop replayed — reproducing the 2026-08-27 figures exactly (**16592** discoveries, **14953** roots, **38** flagged, **670** residual, `simpleContent-forbidden` 416 + `complexContent-forbidden` 168 = **584**, the remaining 86 being the 31 `defaultAttributes` #1046 landed plus the 55 conservative declines) and establishing the fact the original measurement never had, that those 584 discoveries sit in **580 distinct assembled root documents**; #1126's 588 flipped expectation lines attribute to **475 distinct documents** (475 `schema` lines exactly one per document, 113 `instance` lines over 92 of those same 475, histogram 80×1 9×2 1×3 1×4 1×8), and **all 475 fall inside the 580 — 100% containment, zero movers from outside the bucket, and 105 bucket documents that moved nothing** — which is recorded separately as a soundness/upper-bound property and not as a conversion, because *reconciled* required a factor a session can state BEFORE a landing and there is none; the ruling on the residual is the one four `/backlog` stamps have already held — *"read a residual bucket count as a candidate filter and never as a sort key"* — now with a case-by-case reconciliation under it instead of an open question; **arbiter REJECTED round 1** on a stale figure the landing quoted from the issue body it was landing against, **ACCEPTED round 2** with zero findings, `Ratchet: unchanged` run rather than inferred, `surface: unchanged`, **1 file `+49/−18`**, `docs/PLAN.md` alone, no `.go` file and no `conformance/testdata/expectations/` file anywhere in the branch.

  to bank.`** `GOXSD_RATCHET=1 go test ./conformance -run TestConformance

- Decisions made and why:
  - **Coincidence, not reconciled, and the containment recorded separately.**
    Mason offered the alternative reading itself (100% containment as a stated
    conversion) and the arbiter refused it in both rounds: containment is a
    filter property, the near-agreement of the two totals is not, and the yield
    and the fan-out are both post-hoc. Containment is also mechanically what
    you would predict — #1126 changed that very predicate, so nothing outside
    its bucket could move.
  - **An `instance` expectation line is attributed to its group's SCHEMA
    document, not to the instance XML.** That is `censusRoot`'s own definition
    and the unit the 584 was measured in; attributing to the instance file
    gives 113 further documents and a different fan-out, and compares two
    different things. Mason named this as the load-bearing choice and the
    arbiter ruled it right.
  - **The 45-vs-46 gap is stated outright rather than papered over.** The five
    per-class counts #786 left (9 and 9, filed as #1182; 14, 11 and 2 unfiled)
    were measured at `adb6d57` and total 45, while the instrument reports 46 at
    HEAD, so they do not decompose the live figure. Mason declined to assert a
    breakdown it had not measured and asked whether disclosing the gap is the
    *"pre-arguing against a misreading"* CLAUDE.md forbids; the arbiter ruled
    **keep it** — the non-decomposition IS the surprise, and suppressing it
    leaves a reader with 46 above five counts totalling 45 and no signal that
    subtracting them is invalid, which is this issue's own defect one order of
    magnitude smaller.
  - **`STYLE T6` was miscited and there is no ID to substitute.** T6
    (`docs/STYLE.md:145`) governs a package `doc.go`'s status prose, not
    `docs/PLAN.md`; `docs/STYLE.md` has no rule on point, verified by both
    agents independently, so `eedf740`'s body cites WORKFLOW's *"Scope: what
    one landing carries"* three-question test run in order instead. The two
    absorbed *"Next planning action"* repairs — items 2 and 4, both asserting
    #1164 had yet to rule — stand; only the citation moved.
  - **The Working band rows at `docs/PLAN.md:309` were deliberately NOT
    retired**, and both agents drew the line in the same place: the band is a
    stamped `/backlog` snapshot replaced whole, and retiring one row mid-stamp
    misrepresents that pass's ranking. Prose that this landing makes FALSE is
    absorbed; queue ordering is not.
  - **One word taken beyond the two findings**: `:66`'s *"580 distinct schema
    documents"* → *"assembled root documents"*, verdict-invited and
    brief-excluded, resolved toward the verdict. `schema` is a lane name two
    lines below, in the one paragraph whose thesis is that units matter.

## 2026-09-02 — post-land pass for #1164

**ZERO unblocked and the zero is MEASURED, not assumed (#400); ONE issue filed
(#1196); ONE re-scoped against a "do not widen" note it carried (#1171); TWO of
the four follow-ups DISMISSED with reasons; and one open body corrected
(#1182).** The pass's own finding is that the arbiter's first handed-forward
item — *"the unexplained +1"* — is the wrong question, and filing it as asked
would have reproduced the defect #1164 exists to kill. 45 against a live 46 is a
gap only if `670 − 584 − 31 − 10 = 45` is trusted, and that subtraction is what
#1051's residual-arithmetic note and #1164's own ruling both forbid. #1196
therefore owns the **live composition of the 46**, bucketed at HEAD, from which
the +1 either resolves or turns out never to have existed — and its Acceptance
writes *"the totals decompose at HEAD and the 45-vs-46 was an artefact of
comparing two measurement commits"* in as a complete answer rather than a
failure to find something.

- **Unblock sweep: ZERO, and the zero is measured (#400).** All **251** open
  issues fetched over the repository-scoped REST issues endpoint per
  `docs/ROUTINES.md`'s **Survey input** recipe — page-numbered, `pull_request`
  entries dropped, de-duplicated by number — and every `## Depends on` section
  extracted and read. **No open issue names #1164 as a dependency**: zero of
  251, and no `blocked` issue's gate moved. Five open bodies mention the string
  anywhere — #1196 (this pass's own), #1182, #1171, #1051, and #404, where it
  is an incidental match inside an unrelated `kind/gap` on `closureScan`. All
  **19** `blocked` issues re-read: nine name an open issue, seven name a trigger
  and are marked do-not-rescan, #1042 waits on an M6/M7 XPath evaluator, and
  #1051 is below. #1164 was filed `Depends on: none` and gated nothing, exactly
  as its body said.
- **#1051 stays `blocked`, and the ruling is not why.** Stated rather than
  assumed, because the landing sat beside it all week. Two of its three
  dependency bullets are struck as discharged (#414/#438; #1046). The live one
  is the 55 conservative declines, whose own text is the gate — *"three
  buckets, 27 discoveries, are still unfiled, so #786's closure unblocks nothing
  here."* That is a **gate widening, not a verdict**, and #1164 widened no gate
  and filed no bucket, so it moves #1051 in neither direction. No label touched.
  The one thing the ruling gives #1051 is negative: its bar is `residual == 0`,
  and the ruling confirms that bar cannot be reached by arithmetic over the
  `adb6d57` table it currently reads — which is what #1196 supplies.
- **#1078 and #1183 were independent all along.** Both were named at grounding
  as *"downstream consumers of whatever #1164 rules"*; re-read at HEAD, neither

  census-region retirement in `parser/` and cites none of the 584, the 588 or

  constant by induction. Neither carries a stale premise. **No edit to either.**
  The grounding's reading was over-cautious, which is the right direction.
- **#1182's trigger is armed and has NOT fired.** `docs/PLAN.md`'s *Next
  planning action* item 2 reads *"if #1182 lands and `schema` moves by anything
  other than 18, read 18 as the upper bound the ruling says it is."* #1182 is
  **open and unlanded**; informational, nothing to act on. One clause of its

  conflated once already"* pointed at an open question and now points at the
  landed ruling, plus the live 46 and #1196 as the owner of its two counts'
  composition. The instruction it supports (*"do not present 18 as a predicted

  `kind/refactor`, `area/conformance`, `Depends on: none`. The instrument was
  re-run at `2cc7ca6` rather than quoted: **46 residual** over 16592 discoveries

  saw it raised and did not hand it forward**: round 2's *"Handed forward, not
  findings"* names three items and mason's scrutiny item 5 is not among them
  after a full round in front of the judge. That is a ruling by omission and
  this pass does not overturn it. The 105, the 130 cases and the containment

## 2026-09-03 — #404 — **the schema lane's blanket `Unfollowed() > 0 && perr != nil` conjunction is replaced by `fabricatedRejection`, a two-arm predicate that declines only the failures a missing document could actually have fabricated, and the lane banks 13885 → 13896 (+11)**: the oracle ruled the spec defines **no** normative correlation between a specific unresolved `<include>`/`<import>`/`<redefine>`/`<override>` and a downstream `src-resolve` failure — `src-resolve` (§3.17.6.2) tests a QName against the composed schema's aggregate properties with no back-reference to which directive fell short, `src-include`'s retain-and-retry instruction is not blame attribution, and §5.3's ·absent· consequence is generic — so **any** correlation predicate here is an engineering heuristic layered on the cited rules and the old conjunction was spec-consistent-but-coarse rather than a spec violation, which is why the predicate's doc comment says *heuristic* outright and fabricates no rule ID; mason's census (Acceptance item 5, the load-bearing bullet) enumerated **169** schema-lane decline candidates by gate — `closure-undecidable` 59, `extra-docs` 73, `root-unreadable` 9, `root-not-schema` 1, **this conjunction 27** — and split those 27 into **12 decidable** and **15 that must keep declining** (11 real `src-resolve` fabrications, 4 `UnfollowedUnreadable`); the shipped predicate declines if any unfollowed directive's reason is `UnfollowedUnreadable`, else iff `xsderr.RuleOf(perr) == "src-resolve"`, on the **rule axis and not proximity**, because a `Loc` heuristic was tried first and found **backwards** — in the true-hazard cases the `src-resolve` error sits at the referring site while the directive sits at the import site, so different `Loc`s mark the genuinely RELATED pairs; the arbiter **REJECTED** round 1 on a `[STYLE P3a]` finding in the ratchet-corrupting direction — the new doc comment claimed `UnfollowedUnreadable` was the only remaining non-verdict mode when `parser/parse.go:735-737` has a second, recording `UnfollowedLocationUnresolved` for a hard (non-`ErrNotFound`) resolver fault and returning a plain unruled error the predicate would read as a genuine `invalid` — reproduced artificially with `schemaLocation="a.xsd/b.xsd"` (ENOTDIR), zero suite cases exercising it — plus a `[STYLE P2]` undercount of the decline cohort's shapes; **one comment-only repair** filed **#1201** for the residual, marked it at both arms and fixed the count, and **round 2 ACCEPTED with no finding**, `surface: unchanged` at both rounds, 4 files **+229/−59** from base.

- Shipped on `wip/issue-404`, base `origin/main` at **`5e7dd24`**, still
  `origin/main`'s head at landing. Three commits, nothing amended and nothing

- Decisions made and why:
  - **The rule axis, after `Loc` proximity was tried and measured backwards.** The
    grounding named proximity as the fallback the design had to cover; mason
    covered it by falsifying it. In the true-hazard cases the `src-resolve` error
    is charged at the referring site and the directive sits at the import site, so
    same-`Loc` marks the UNRELATED pairs — the signal inverted. `src-resolve` is
    the only rule this producer charges for an unresolvable reference, which the
    arbiter re-derived independently rather than taking.
  - **`UnfollowedUnreadable` kept as its own rule-blind arm.** A read failure does
    not separate a well-formedness fault from a reader limitation — step 1's own
    reasoning for the root document — and collapsing the two arms into one rule
    test would have decided `iri-001`, a case the suite calls valid.
  - **The heuristic is documented AS a heuristic, no rule ID minted for it.** The
    oracle's ruling is that no spec text licenses the correlation; a fabricated
    `Spec:` citation would have been the failure mode here.
  - **#1201 filed, not fixed, and explicitly NOT blocking.** The residual is a
    pre-existing gap in `parser/`, outside this issue's diff and outside
    `conformance/` altogether; option 1 (a new `UnfollowedReason` member) is
    exported `parser` surface against `## Surface: none expected`, and folding the
    hard fault into the `ErrNotFound` no-op changes `parser.Parse`/`ParseReport`
    for every consumer. Neither belongs in a repair round.
  - **`closureGateIn` now calls `fabricatedRejection` instead of restating it**,
    removing a T4 duplicate that could have drifted from the executor.

  - **The census's own prediction held to the case**: 12 decidable of 27, eleven

    declared at ?)` — the `?` is the seeded builtin's absent `Loc`.
    `msData/additional/test65699.xsd` is a copy of the schema for schema documents
    carrying `targetNamespace="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema"`, so it declares
    `anyType` itself, and the suite's intended fault is elsewhere entirely
    (*"invalid partical derivation by restriction"*, in `test65699.inc`).

    `test73722.xsd` and `test65026.xsd`, the other two schema documents in the

    `<expected validity="indeterminate"/>`, `status="stable"`, citing W3C Bugzilla

    independent intended fault, which is why the WG marked it `invalid` and not
    `indeterminate`.

## 2026-09-03 — post-land pass for #471

**ZERO unblocked and the zero is measured; NOTHING filed and the reason is
stated; FIVE bodies corrected, of which the load-bearing one is #1205's
Acceptance — it had guessed a footing for the inline path
(`e-props-correct` "clause 3, or its `final` analogue") that DOES NOT EXIST,
which is the same fabricated-verdict trap #471's own arbiter rejected round 1
for, reproduced inside the follow-up mason filed during that repair round; ONE
untracked collision found and named on both its sides (#1205 × #768); ONE
adjacent uncharged rule recorded as a scope boundary rather than absorbed or
filed (`src-element` clause 4).** `docs/PLAN.md` untouched, deliberately, on the
2026-09-01 precedent.

- **Unblock sweep: ZERO, measured.** **No open issue's `## Depends on` names
  #471**, and none of the **20** open `blocked` issues mentions it anywhere in a
  body. Feed taken by REST straight to files — `state=open`, `per_page=100`,
  pages requested one at a time because `--paginate` switches to numeric-ID
  repository paths on page 2 and this proxy refuses those (HTTP 403); pages of
  100/100/53, **253 open non-PR issues**. Exactly **seven** open bodies contain
  `#471` — **#1205**, **#1206**, **#1136**, **#1135**, **#731**, **#1171**,
  **#613** — and not one of them is `blocked`. #471 was filed
  `Depends on: none` and nothing was ever queued behind it. Eight of the twenty
  `blocked` issues carry an explicit *"a trigger, not an issue — do not re-scan
  this dependency list on the next unblock sweep"* paragraph and were honoured,
  not re-scanned. Written down because a measured zero and a skipped sweep are
  otherwise indistinguishable from `git log` (#400).
- **#1205 and #1206 verified, and both were wrong in ways worth a session.**
  The scope decision that carved the ten attributes out of #471 was NOT
  relitigated — it is settled, recorded in the landing entry and on the thread,
  and both issues remain `ready` / `kind/gap` / `area/parser` / M4 with labels,
  milestone and titles untouched.
  - **#1205 — the invented rule.** Acceptance read *"likely `e-props-correct`
    clause 3 (or its `final` analogue) for the inline path, mirroring
    `substitutionGroup`'s existing charge there"*. Read against the spec rather
    than the account: `e-props-correct` (§3.3.6.1, `xmlschema11-1.md:1520`) has
    clauses **1-7** and not one confines `{abstract}` or `{substitution group
    exclusions}` by `{scope}.{variety}` — **clause 3 is specific to
    `{substitution group affiliations}`**, which is exactly why
    `produceLocalElement` may charge it for `substitutionGroup`
    (`produce_complex.go:2200`) and exactly why it does not generalize to these
    two names. `src-element` supplies nothing on the inline path either: clause
    2.2's antecedent is *"If `ref` is present"* and clause 2.1 is the ref/name
    exclusivity. What remains is the uncataloged §5.1 grammar class carrying no
    rule ID — the footing `rejectProhibitedAttrs` already stands on, whose doc
    at `parser/produce.go:1205-1213` calls charging `e-props-correct` there a
    fabricated verdict (STYLE E2) in as many words. Acceptance now says this,
    per path, and still routes the ruling through grounding rather than
    settling it here. **The ref= half of the hint was already right and is
    unchanged.** Spec cite `:5110-5130` checked against the tree: exactly the
    `xs:localElement` complexType, prohibited trio at `:5124-5126`.
  - **#1206 — three sharpenings, no substantive fault.** Its fixture list,
    ratchet warning and `elemL003`-must-not-regress caution are the strongest
    part of the body and were left as filed. (i) The `:5044-5085` cite holds
    seven of the eight attributes inline; **`name` is not among them** — it
    arrives via `xs:attributeGroup ref="xs:defRef"` at `:5065`, `xs:defRef`
    declared at `:4636-4644` — so a session grepping the cited range for a
    `name` declaration finds nothing and could read that silence backwards.
    (ii) **`name` is the one of the eight where clause 2.1 also bites**, and the

## 2026-09-03 — #1181 — **`simpleContentExtensionDecidable`, `simpleContentRestrictionDecidable` and `contentDecidable` stop declining the particle-child and stray-wrapper forms, and `schema` banks 157 → 141 declines (−16): the producer already REJECTS every one of those shapes as an s4s order fault, so both godocs' "silently drops" mechanism was false and the declines were conservative rather than forced — this family's FIRST round-1 accept, and the difference is that mason probed every mechanism sentence it wrote instead of inheriting one**: the whole change is the three predicates widened to admit the s4s-rejected children plus the four prose sites and two witnesses those declines carried, every shape re-probed per-position through `parser.ParseReport` rather than taken from the issue's premise — because this predicate family had two prior round-1 rejects (#786, #1182) for exactly the unverified "drops in silence" claim this issue exists to correct, and the arbiter reproduced all four probes independently, ran the mutation control itself, and confirmed the census invariant change; the s4s fault is charged under **§2.4 clause 1 `sd-valid` (`xmlschema11-1.md:615`), restated at §5.1's first bullet (:4296), no numbered rule ID**, against `xs:simpleExtensionType` (:1697) and `xs:simpleRestrictionType` (:1692) for the simpleContent positions, `xs:complexRestrictionType` (:1718) and **`xs:extensionType` (:1723 — NOT "xs:complexExtensionType", which the grounding found does not exist in the spec)** for `contentDecidable`'s complexContent positions, and `xs:complexTypeModel` (:1649) for the implicit-content position; the ONE shape that stays declined is a plural-named `<assertions>` child, genuinely accepted-but-inert — `s4sFacetElement` admits it (its bridge keyed by facet *names*, and §4.3.13 spells the facet in the plural), then `restrictionFacets` matches only the singular `<assertion>` and `facetKindOf` excludes `xsd.FacetAssertions`, so it folds into no component — captured by renaming `facetElement` → `silentlyDroppedFacetElement` with the semantics inverted; `schema_closure_test.go`'s `undecidable` witness const was repointed to a top-level unknown-name shape (a real silent top-level skip) and proven load-bearing by the standard `schemaShapeDecidable` → `return true` mutation, and `census_test.go`'s `TestUnmappedCensusSoundAgainstShapeGate` had its exemption widened from `holdsMisplacedNotation`-only to "the assembly was rejected" (`perr != nil`), since a rejected assembly can fabricate no false accept; **ARBITER ACCEPT in round 1, zero findings**, `datatypes` and `instance` unchanged, `surface: unchanged`, 6 files **+220/−148** from base.

  ctE011 ctE012 ctE013 ctE014`; `idA023 idB023 idC023` — byte-identical across

  demanded every mechanism sentence be probed before it is written, mason probed

  (#881 — keep the gate uniform, ~280–440s of it each) are the two documented
  costs, one clause each.
- Decisions made and why:
  - **Widen by NAME against a per-position probe, never the default arm
    wholesale.** `contentDecidable`'s arm is reachable at three positions with
    three different grammars; mason probed each and admitted only the shapes each
    grammar rejects, leaving the one genuine silent drop declined. This is the
    trap the issue body named, and skipping it is what the two prior rejects did.
  - **The plural `<assertions>` decline was KEPT**, re-measured at the position it
    governs: `<assertions>` reaches the facet slot, matches no singular
    `<assertion>`, and folds into nothing — accepted-but-inert, so its silence is
    a real missing rejection, not a conservative decline. Only the particle and
    stray-wrapper clauses were false. The `facetElement` → `silentlyDroppedFacetElement`
    rename inverts the predicate's sense so its one caller reads true only for
    that surviving case.
  - **The `census_test.go` invariant, not its data, was the one assertion
    changed — flagged for the closest look and upheld.** The newly-admitted names
    are correctly `UnmappedNoDispatch`; the old test asserted `flagged ⟹
    !decidable` with a `holdsMisplacedNotation`-only exemption, and the widening
    made those names decidable-and-flagged. Repointing the exemption to "the
    assembly was rejected" (`perr != nil`) is sound because the census reports a
    name whether or not another pass rejects the document over it, and non-vacuous
    because the 14-of-38 flagged names in ACCEPTED assemblies still carry the full
    `flagged ⟹ !decidable` assertion.
- Surprises:
  - **The grounding corrected two citations the issue itself carried.**
    `xs:complexExtensionType` is a name the local specs do not use — the real type
    is `xs:extensionType`, shared between complexContent's `<extension>` and (by
    restriction) `xs:simpleExtensionType`'s base — and the issue's "only singular
    `<assert>`" is imprecise: the Part 2 facet element is singular `<assertion>`,
    `<assert>` being the distinct Part 1 structures-level element, both easily
    confused with the non-existent plural `<assertions>` ELEMENT. Both corrections
    are carried into the prose rather than left on the thread.
  - **The complexContent restriction and extension grammars are related but not
    byte-identical** — `xs:extensionType` permits `openContent` with no following
    particle where `xs:complexRestrictionType`'s Appendix A form makes the particle
    mandatory once its wrapping group is entered — which is why "probe each
    position" was the right instruction and an analogy would not have been.
- Friction — **nothing this session paid that the documents did not already
  predict.** The two documented costs (#659 submodule init, #881 uniform gate)
  are ruled and stated above in one clause each. The one pattern worth naming for
  `/retro` is a *positive*: #1182's Friction called this family's false-mechanism
  rejects a two-landing pattern with "nothing binds a mechanism sentence a mason
  writes mid-implementation" and no process owner, entry point *implementation*.
  #1181's body then wrote that discipline into its own Notes ("probe every
  mechanism sentence you write into a comment here"), mason followed it, and the
  landing accepted clean — evidence that the per-issue note worked where a general
  rule did not yet exist. Whether that argues for promoting the discipline out of
  one issue body and into a process document, or leaves it as a per-issue note the
  cartographer adds where the hazard recurs, is the ruling a `/retro` on this
  family owes; the tree-side residue is closed, the process side is where the
  open question sits.

## 2026-09-04 — #1206 — **`src-element` clause 2.2's prose-only eight are charged on a local `<element ref="...">` and the `schema` lane banks 13912 → 13928 (+16) — ONE MORE than the 15 predicted, because `targetNamespace`'s only fixture in the whole W3C suite is UTF-16LE and every UTF-8 grep over `testdata/xsdtests` reads it as interleaved NULs**: that file — `ibmData/schema_invalid/S3_2_3/s3_2_3si10.xsd`, suite key `constraintsOnAttribute/s3_2_3si10`, holding `<element ref="b:element1InB" minOccurs="0" targetNamespace="..."/>` at line 12 — falsifies this issue's own *"zero fixtures found at grounding time — re-verify"* and, with it, **fires the reopening condition of the `src-element` clause 4 (`ed-with-ns`) tracker that 2026-09-03's `/backlog` DISMISSED at `docs/PLAN.md:287-296` on exactly that zero** (*"It becomes fileable the moment a fixture surfaces"*), which is the one consequence of this landing that reaches past its own diff; the executable change is a single unexported `rejectRefElementDeclarationAttrs(el *Element) error` walking a fixed array literal first-hit-wins in `xs:element`'s OWN declaration order — `name` reached through `<xs:attributeGroup ref="xs:defRef"/>` at `:5065` and declared at `:4642`, then `type`, `default`, `fixed`, `nillable`, `block`, `form`, `targetNamespace` inline at `:5066`-`:5082` (STYLE D2: the attribute the rejection names is output, so it never follows document order or a map's) — wired into `elementParticleTerm`'s `ref=` branch between `rejectRefElementSubstitutionGroup` and `rejectRefElementChildren` so clause 2.2's two attribute charges are contiguous and its child half is unambiguously last; the grounding **RULED** `name` under clause 2.2 rather than the clause 2.1 that also bites it, and `targetNamespace` under 2.2 rather than the clause 4.1 that also bites it, because the spec adjudicates NO precedence between clauses that fire together — §B leaves the tabulation of error conditions deliberately incomplete at `xmlschema11-1.md:6925` — so one attribute draws one rule ID from one helper and both rulings are recorded in its doc comment rather than falling out of statement order; **this session RESUMED an EXPIRED `wip/issue-1206`**, whose GROUNDING and MASON rounds an earlier interrupted session had already pushed, so its own contribution is the Judge step and this Land step; the arbiter **ACCEPTED at round 1 with ZERO findings** on a re-verification that took nothing from mason's account — all four gate parts re-run, every spec cite re-read at the line, the tests mutation-tested rather than read, and the fixture census re-taken **UTF-16-aware** BEFORE the ratchet was written — `Ratchet: schema +16`, `surface: unchanged`, **3 files, +167/−29**.

- Cost — **1 oracle, 1 mason, 1 arbiter.** Zero repair rounds, zero rejects, no
  warden (`surface: unchanged`), no cartographer. **Split across two sessions:
  the oracle and mason rounds were the earlier, interrupted one's; this session
  paid the arbiter round and this landing.** What the single round bought is the
  16th case: mason predicted **15** and said so before running, the arbiter
  declined to accept the table on its face and ran its own **UTF-16-aware**
  census — **19 documents** carrying an unqualified `ref=` plus one of the eight,
  16 of them flipping — so the prediction miss was diagnosed to a decoding
  assumption rather than absorbed as noise, and the correction it produced
  reaches `docs/PLAN.md`'s dismissal (see Next 2). The dominant cost remains
  conformance: four full suite runs at 218–376s, plus the ratchet write. The
  four-part gate on a two-file `.go` diff is ruled (#881 — keep the gate
  uniform); the submodule init per fresh container is documented (#659) and was
  run at both rounds, suite at `7bc3365`.
- Decisions made and why:
  - **`name` charged under clause 2.2, not clause 2.1** — the ruling the
    cartographer's post-land pass on #471 demanded be *made* rather than
    inherited from statement order. The oracle established the spec is silent:
    a full-file grep of `src-element` returns the definition at `:1320` and two
    TOC refs and no precedence rule anywhere, and `:6925` says the tabulation of
    error conditions *"has not been completed... no further work in this area is
    currently anticipated"*. So it is an engineering choice, and the choice is
    uniformity — one rule ID, one helper, eight attributes. **Clause 2.1 is still
    charged nowhere in the tree**, and that stays true after this landing.
  - **`targetNamespace` likewise under 2.2, not clause 4.1**, on the identical
    reasoning, which is what the issue's own Notes asked for.
  - **One helper looping an array literal, not eight `if` blocks or eight
    siblings.** The alternative mirrors `rejectRefElementSubstitutionGroup`
    literally and costs eight copies of one sentence, and it makes the declared
    order an invisible property of statement sequence instead of a readable list.
    The array is inline rather than a package-level `var`, matching
    `produce.go:1731` and avoiding a mutable global.
  - **The array, not the element's own attribute list.** Iterating what the
    document carries would report the first attribute the parser happened to see;
    D2 makes the reported name output, and output is deterministic.
  - **Placed between the two existing helpers**, not after both. The spec
    licenses any order among the three, and this keeps the attribute charges
    adjacent. It is one of the sites **#1136** moves if it ever rules an order —
    which is the run-order question, untouched here: the s4s walk still answers
    first.
  - **`rejectRefElementDeclarationAttrs` is a coinage, and the arbiter let it
    stand.** "Declaration attributes" is not a spec term; it names the attributes
    only a DECLARING `<element>` can carry, and the doc comment says so.
  - **The `GAP(xsd)` block above `rejectRefElementSubstitutionGroup` was rewritten
    rather than left aspirational** — its count drops from ten to two, it points
    the eight at the new helper, and it now names only `final`/`abstract` with
    **#1205** as owner. The arbiter confirmed #1205 open and confirmed
    independently that those two really are charged nowhere, since
    `rejectProhibitedAttrs` is reached only via `formTopLevel`
    (`produce.go:943,956,984,997`) and never for a local element (STYLE P3).
- Surprises:
  - **A UTF-16 fixture is invisible to the whole toolchain of greps this repo
    reasons with.** `s3_2_3si10.xsd` is `XML 1.0 document, Unicode text, UTF-16,
    little-endian`; #471's grounding, this issue's body and mason's own
    prediction scan all read it as interleaved NULs and all reported zero
    `targetNamespace` fixtures. It is the second instrument-blindness finding in
    two weeks after #815's `paragraph()`/`citations` pair, and the same shape:
    the measurement was wrong in a way the measurer could not see.
  - **The prediction missed LOW, which is the safe direction and still a miss.**
    A high miss would have meant an unexplained flip at bank time; a low one meant
    an unbanked case surfaced by `-v`. Both are accounted for case by case here.
  - **`elemL003`'s reported rule changed and no lane can see it.** It was `pass`
    before and after, because its `<xsd:element name="foo" ref="foo"/>` sits in an
    `xs:all` with no top-level `foo` and was already rejected by `src-resolve` on
    the unresolvable `ref=`; it is now rejected earlier, by this helper, on
    `name`. The schema lane records pass/fail against declared validity and never
    compares rule IDs. Mason flagged it rather than passing over it and the
    arbiter agreed with the outcome — the grammar fault sits closer to the source
    than the resolution failure — but **it is a behaviour change the ratchet is
    structurally unable to observe**, and the only record of it is this entry and
    the thread.
  - **`name00505m2` is the case that would have caught a scoping bug, and it
    correctly did not move.** Its `<xsd:element ref="Global2" type="xsd:string">`
    is a direct child of `<xsd:schema>`, so clause 2's *"if the item's parent is
    not `<schema>`"* precondition excludes it and `elementParticleTerm` never runs
    on it — the helper's precondition is satisfied by the call site alone. Had the
    check been hoisted to `produceElement` the count would read **17**, not 16.
    The arbiter opened the file rather than reasoning about it.
  - **`addB045` IS `test72794`**, closing the last unmapped name in the issue
    body's fixture list.

  `.claude/commands/develop.md:31-36` sends a resuming session to *"read the

**ZERO unblocked and the zero is measured; THREE issues filed — #1215
(`src-element` clause 4, `ed-with-ns`), #1216 (`src-attribute` clause 6,
`att-with-ns`, which nobody had ever looked at) and #1217 (the survey-input
gitignore leak) — and #1136 re-stamped in BOTH its body and a comment; the
pass's own finding is that the dismissal `docs/PLAN.md` item 4 recorded was
wrong by SIX cases and not the one #1206 found, because decoding
`ibmData/schema_invalid/S3_2_3/` turns up three element-side and three
attribute-side `fail` fixtures for two constraints charged nowhere in the tree,
every one of them accepted by the parser today.**

  fetched over `gh api repos/kud360/goxsd8/issues` (`state=open`,
  `per_page=100`, paged explicitly to exhaustion, PRs filtered by
  `.pull_request == null`) and every one of the **19** `blocked` bodies had its
  `## Depends on` section extracted and read. **No blocked issue names #1206 as
  a dependency.** The string `1206` appears in exactly **three** open bodies —
  **#1205**, **#1136** and **#1135** — and all three are `ready`, not `blocked`,
  and were `ready` before this landing; each names #1206 as adjacent work, none
  as a gate. #1206 itself was filed `Depends on: none`. **Zero relabelled**, and
  the count is the same 19-blocked shape the last two passes measured, six of
  which say in as many words that no landing can unblock them.
- **#1215 FILED — `src-element` clause 4 (`ed-with-ns`), `ready`,
  `kind/gap`, `area/parser`, M4 — and the ruling it rests on is NOT the one
  #1206's `Next` 2 anticipated.** `Next` 2 handed this pass a judgment call:
  the dismissal's first ground (*"the ref= overlap has zero suite fixtures"*)
  was false, its other two stood, and filing was *"cartographer's call to make
  on the evidence, not an automatic filing"*. The call was made against fresh
  evidence rather than against those three grounds, and the evidence moved.
  - **The `ref=` overlap is the WRONG basis for filing and #1215 excludes it.**
    `constraintsOnAttribute/s3_2_3si10` — the fixture that fired the reopening
    condition — is `pass` as of `e70c1ae`, charged under clause **2.2** by
    #1206's own `rejectRefElementDeclarationAttrs`. A clause-4 tracker built on
    it would name a green case and move no lane. Had the scan stopped there the
    honest disposition would have been to re-dismiss.
  - **It did not stop there.** `s3_2_3si10` is UTF-16LE, so the whole directory
    is invisible to the greps this repo reasons with; decoding every `.xsd`
    under `ibmData/schema_{invalid,valid}/S3_2_3/` found **six more**
    `targetNamespace`-on-a-local-declaration fixtures, all at `fail` in
    `conformance/testdata/expectations/schema.txt`. Three are element-side and
    are #1215's: **`s3_2_3si01`** (`element name="e" ... targetNamespace="b"`
    with **no `restriction` ancestor at all** — clause 4.3.2),
    **`s3_2_3si06`** (`form="qualified"` alongside `targetNamespace` — clause
    **4.2**) and **`s3_2_3si08`** (`restriction base="xsd:anyType"` — clause
    4.3.2 again, the arm the fixture's own comment names). **On the `name=`
    form clause 2.2's *"If `ref` is present"* antecedent never fires**, so
    clause 4 is the only footing and #1206's landing does not touch them.
  - **Verified, not inferred.** `go run ./cmd/goxsd8 parse` was run on each of
    the three at `1780670`: all three are ACCEPTED, printing a summary, against
    a suite that marks them schema-invalid. This is under-rejection, not a
    disagreement about which rule to report — the strongest shape a `kind/gap`
    filing can have.
  - **`grep -rn 'ed-with-ns' --include=*.go .` returns nothing**, and the one
    tree mention of the constraint (`parser/produce.go:1209`) names it precisely

## 2026-09-04 — #1188 — **`parse`'s three contract copies now attribute the `namespace:` lines to the COMPILATION — the argument document and every one it includes, imports, overrides or redefines — and the round-1 wording that attributed them to the argument document alone was already falsified by a pin sitting in the same test file: `importsSummary` (`cmd/goxsd8/parse_test.go:32-46`, bought by #472's arbiter) prints `namespace: http://example.com/imported`, which no component the argument document declares is in**: the defect is in the documents and it landed as documentation and tests only — `git diff origin/main -- cmd/goxsd8/parse.go` is **empty** at both commits, re-verified by the arbiter after every mutation experiment — because §3.17.1's Schema component genuinely has **no `{target namespace}` property**, the grounding quoting the eight-property tableau whole at `xmlschema11-1.md:3762` with §2.2's *"Several kinds of component have a target namespace"* (`:405`) and §3.17.2's *"none of the attribute information items displayed above correspond directly to properties of schemas"* (`:3792`) corroborating, so `namespacesOf` deriving the lines from declared components is the only derivation the abstract model offers and the contract's **silence about that derivation**, not the derivation, is what the cliuser hit; Acceptance **(b)** over (a) as the grounding ruled, so no `xsd` accessor was invented for `cmd` to be its only consumer (STYLE 8) and no warden pre-flight was owed, `surface: unchanged` being the cheap tell the issue body itself designed; the arbiter **REJECTED round 1** on two blocking findings and one advisory, every one about SCOPE rather than about spec — a false contrast within one sentence (namespaces attributed to what *it* declares, counts to what *the schema documents* make, both read off the same buckets), no pin for the composition half, and a negative-only assertion this file's house style does not use — and **ACCEPTED round 2 with zero findings** after verifying all four composition mechanisms empirically rather than from `parser/doc.go`'s enumeration; `Ratchet: unchanged` run and not inferred at **both** rounds, `surface: unchanged`, **4 files, +93/−8**, and no line of production logic anywhere in the branch.

- Cost — **1 oracle, 2 mason (1 repair), 2 arbiter (1 reject).** No warden
  (`surface: unchanged`), no cartographer, no steward. What the second round
  bought is the difference between a contract that is true and one that is true
  only under the more generous of two readings: round 1's sentence was
  **falsifiable in two lines of XSD**, and the arbiter falsified it twice — once
  against a fixture already in the tree (`testdata/imports.xsd`) and once
  against a `targetNamespace`'d zero-component root `<xs:include>`ing a
  component-bearing document, which differs from the landing's own new fixture

  dominant cost is conformance: **each of the four rounds ran the suite twice**
  — gate part 1's `go test ./...` and part 4's `-v` pass — at **272–345s** where
  a figure was reported, and **each arbiter round added a ratchet run** on top,
  all of it on a diff whose only production content is prose. That is the
  uniform gate, ruled (#881), and not even the doc-only case that ruling covers,
  since three of the four files are `.go`. The submodule init per fresh
  container is documented (#659) and both mason and the arbiter ran it.
- Decisions made and why:
  - **Acceptance (b), and (a) ruled out before anything was written.** (a) needs
    a new exported accessor on `xsd.Schema` whose only consumer would be `cmd`
    (STYLE 8) and whose return shape for a set assembled from documents with
    different `targetNamespace` values is a design question the issue does not
    carry — a warden pre-flight this landing then never needed. The grounding
    ruled the spec licenses neither direction and constrains both identically,
    so the choice is engineering and the cheap direction wins.
  - **All four composition mechanisms are named, not "includes or imports".**
    `parser/doc.go:124` states the closure — *"Parse assembles the
    `<xs:include>`, `<xs:override>`, `<xs:redefine>` and `<xs:import>` closure
    of the root document"* — so a contract stopping at include/import would be a
    smaller copy of the defect this issue exists to fix. The arbiter did not
    take that on the citation: it built four roots that each declare **zero**
    components and confirmed each mechanism alone produces the namespace line
    and the count.
  - **"the compilation" is the block's own word**, taken from `several arguments
    are several compilations, not one set` three lines below. "Set" was
    unavailable precisely because in this block it already means the thing
    several arguments are *not*.
  - **No fourth sentence explaining §3.17.1's negative fact.** The contract
    blocks are terse and `parse.go`'s `summarize` doc comment already carries
    the spec reasoning for a reader who wants it; one clause per copy stating
    the observable rule is what a script author needs (CLAUDE.md, Writing).
    Spelling out that a missing line means *declared nothing* while
    `namespace: (absent)` means *declared something namespaceless* was
    considered and refused for the same reason — the reader derives it.
  - **Both fixtures pin the whole stdout block**, in this file's house style
    alongside `orderSummary` and `importsSummary`, rather than asserting the
    absence of a substring. The advisory was cheap to take in the repair round
    and the arbiter's third mutation — `summarize` emitting the header and
    nothing else — fails **both** namespace tests where the round-1 negative
    assertion would have slept through it.
  - **The Acceptance bullet's literal words were CORRECTED, not followed.**
    (b) says the lines are *"the namespaces of the components the schema
    **document** DECLARED"*; the arbiter ruled at round 1 that the body's own
    phrasing carries the same document-level slip and is the thing to fix rather
    than to copy. **#1188 closes with that phrasing wrong in its body**; the
    correction lives here, in the two verdicts and in the commit body.
  - **`first-appearance order` is preserved and nothing reaches for sorting**,
    per the issue's Notes; the reflow of all three copies was constrained to
    keep `several` ending its line so **`TestUsageCoversContract` passes
    unmodified**, as does `TestBuiltBinaryMatrix` against the real binary.
    **#398**, which owns that test, is named related-not-gating in the body and
    is untouched.
- Surprises:
  - **Mason NAMED the defect in its own round-1 scrutiny list, offered the fix
    wording, and shipped the weaker sentence anyway** — while the pin that
    settles it sat forty lines above the test it was writing. Scrutiny item 1
    asked whether *"it declares"* covers composed components and said that if it
    does not, *"the clause needs 'it or its included and imported documents
    declare' and all three copies move together"*, which is within a word of
    what the repair landed. The flag is the process working; what it exposes is
    that the *cheapest possible check* — reading the file's own pins — was not
    the one run, and a mutation was run instead.
  - **The mirror case is the one nobody predicted.** The issue is filed on a
    `targetNamespace`'d zero-component schema printing NO namespace line; the
    round-1 sentence was falsified by the same schema plus one `<xs:include>`,
    which prints one. `TestParseNamespaceThroughInclusion`
    (`parse_test.go:442`) is that case, and it is the only one of the two new
    tests that catches the wrong reading — the arbiter proved this with its own
    mutation, a `Loc().URI`-filtered bucket feeding `namespacesOf` while leaving
    the counts unfiltered, under which `TestParseNamespaceWithNoComponents`
    **passes**: a fixture whose correct output carries no namespace line cannot
    detect a missing one.
  - **The converse was probed and does not exist.** A compilation that declares
    something and prints no namespace line would be a mirror of the original
    defect; there is none, because anonymous nested types are not counted
    (`anon.xsd` → `types: 0`, `elements: 1`, one namespace line), so
    `namespacesOf`'s anonymous-component skip cannot produce a nonzero count
    with zero namespaces. The sentence holds in both directions.
  - **`<xs:override>` and `<xs:redefine>` really do contribute**, and no
    `cmd/goxsd8/testdata` fixture exercises either — they are exactly the two
    mechanisms a reader could not otherwise predict, which is the argument for
    enumerating rather than gesturing.
  - **`parse.go`'s own `summarize` doc comment still reads per-document** —
    *"a document's targetNamespace is observable only through the components it
    declares"* — and is byte-identical to `origin/main`. It is not one of the
    three contract copies and its governing first sentence already scopes the
    derivation correctly, so nothing was repaired there; recorded, on the
    arbiter's own note, so a later session does not read it as drift.

## 2026-09-04 — post-land pass for #1188

**ZERO unblocked and the zero is measured; ONE issue filed — #1220, the
`## Acceptance` bullet ruling's missing truth question — and it is filed
DELIBERATELY AGAINST the #1188 landing entry's own routing of it, because the
routing was to a channel that wakes nothing.** The landing entry banded the gap
as *"a candidate for the next `/retro`'s process audit and NOT filed as
`kind/process` on one sighting (PRINCIPLES 27)"*; PRINCIPLES 27 governs building
a **tool** for repetitive work, and what this pass filed is a **question**,
whose own Acceptance permits the answer *"no, `satisfiable` subsumes it — close
as decided"*. The repo's standing ruling on the disposition itself runs the
other way and is explicit at four sites: **#291** established that a process
item is filed *for* a `/retro` to rank rather than *by* it; **#615** was filed
at #480's post-land pass deliberately against that landing's own *"This is a
process fix, so no issue is filed; it belongs in the same `/retro`"*, citing
#464/#611 one landing earlier as the same argument already overruled once; #548,
#692 and #925 each say it in their own `## Depends on`; and **#609** exists
because the alternative failed, recording an item carried in `docs/LOG` "Next"
blocks for **five consecutive landings** without ever being filed. The mechanism
is what settles it — `.claude/agents/chronicler.md`'s retro duty reads *"An
issue `blocked` on the next `/retro` is waiting on you, and nothing else wakes
it"*, so a `blocked`-on-retro issue is the retro's mandated input and a log-entry
banding is not. `docs/LOG` is append-only (#382), so the landing entry's framing
stands as written and #1220 is the correction, recorded where a retro can check
it. **`docs/PLAN.md` is untouched.**

  #609's subject** (an instruction carried without being filed) and a second
  sighting of the divergence belongs on #609's thread rather than in a new
  issue. Filing an issue about whether to file issues on one sighting, on one
  sighting, is the accretion the retro exists to prevent.

## 2026-09-04 — #720 — **`goxsd8 validate` ships and spends a FOURTH exit code — `3`, a `-schema` set that does not compile, placed ABOVE `2` in the severity order `max()` reads — and the arbiter's single reject landed on the one thing mason had asked to be scrutinized hardest and had said outright it could not prove: *"I did not prove no input reaches a wrapper-cited error."* Input does, through the DEFAULT configuration, against a `-schema` set that compiles perfectly — an instance whose own advisory `xsi:schemaLocation` names a mis-paired or a malformed document answered `3` at `goxsd8-schema-set.xsd:1:236`, the synthesized wrapper root, a document the user never wrote and cannot open — collapsing the two faults the new code exists to separate, in the direction that sends the operator to the wrong file (STYLE E3, and not a nit: it defeats this issue's headline Acceptance bullet)**: the executable core is `compileSet`, an in-memory namespace-less `<xs:schema>` that `<xs:import>`s every `-schema` document declaring a target namespace of its own and `<xs:include>`s every one declaring none, served through `loader.Chain(loader.Map{…}, loader.Dir(…))` under the bare location `goxsd8-schema-set.xsd` and handed to the ordinary `parser.Parse`, so §4.2.1's `schema(D)` — and with it every cross-document rule the assembly already enforces — holds over the CLI's set; the include/import split is **FORCED in both directions** and the arbiter proved it rather than reading it, `src-include` clause 2 (`xmlschema11-1.md:4065`) offering only 2.1/2.2/2.3, none of which a namespaced document `<include>`d from a namespace-less wrapper satisfies, and `src-import` clause 1.2 (`:4243`) barring a namespace-less `<import>` from that same wrapper, with a forced-`<include>` mutation failing ten tests under the parser's own `[src-include]`; the alternative — compile each `-schema` separately and union the finalized `*xsd.Schema` values — was refused because it needs a merge the library does not offer and would silently drop `sch-props-correct` clause 2 on a name collision and every cross-document reference at finalize; **every instance is assessed in argument order and the run never stops at the first invalid one**, mutation-checked by both mason and the arbiter with a `break` on non-zero; `-format` takes `xml`, `json` and `ber` case-sensitively, an unrecognized token and an extension naming none of the three are usage errors listing the values, and `json`/`ber` exit `2` as reserved-but-unbuilt rather than passing silently; hints are read off the document element through an `xmltree.Reader` over an `io.TeeReader` and replayed by `io.MultiReader`, which is what lets a hinted instance arrive on **stdin**, and `-no-hints` turns them off; **no cvc-/src- rule is implemented anywhere in the branch** — the oracle found §5.2 declining jurisdiction in as many words (*"It is up to applications to decide what constitutes a successful outcome of validation"*) — and **`Ratchet: unchanged` is measured, not assumed**: `go list -deps ./conformance | grep -c cmd/goxsd8` is `0`, so no lane can see this package at all. `surface: +2 -0`, both justified; - Decisions made and why:
  - **The repair took the arbiter's SECOND option — fall back to the base set for
    that instance and report the unusable hint on stderr — not the first (charge
    the instance `exitInvalid`).** The second makes the three hint-failure classes
    ONE behaviour rather than two, which is the self-inconsistency the verdict's
    own point 3 named; the first would have left a mis-paired hint reaching a
    different code from a missing one. `exitSchema` now answers a `-schema` set
    that does not compile **and nothing else**, in those words, in all three
    contract copies (`doc.go:136`, `main.go` usage, `README.md:114`).
  - **`hintFault` elides on `Loc.URI == schemaSetLocation`, a location test, not a
    scan of the message** (`validate.go:287-293`). The split that matters is
    causal by construction and never inspects the error at all: `runValidate`
    compiles `docs` alone first and returns `exitSchema` if that fails, so
    `validatorFor` is only ever reached with a set that already compiled, and
    `augmented` differs from it by exactly this instance's hinted documents over
    the same backend and the same deterministic parse. `hintFault` decides
    *rendering* only, so a misfire there could not collapse the split. No real
    document can collide with the bare name either — every `-schema` path goes
    through `rootLocation` and every hint through `schemaloc.Resolve` against an
    absolute instance path.
  - **`exitSchema = 3` stays ABOVE `exitUsage = 2` and the churn was refused.**
    Mason offered to move it; the arbiter ruled keep, on the const comment's own
    argument — a schema fault leaves *every* instance unassessed rather than any
    one decided — and it is written into all three copies. Do not re-open it.
  - **No copy says exit 0 means "valid."** `doc.go:41` and `usage` say *"Exit 0
    when no instance was charged a violation"*, README *"0 no violation
    charged"* — chosen against `validate/doc.go:101`'s *"An empty Violations with
    a non-empty Unevaluated is not a pass either"*. That wording is exactly what
    makes the dropped `Unevaluated()` a follow-up rather than a blocker.
  - **An extension naming none of `.xml`/`.json`/`.ber` is a usage error naming
    the values, not a guess.** `-` has no extension, so `validate -schema S.xsd -`
    requires `-format xml` — one rule, no stdin special case in the logic. **`-schema
    -` is unsupported**, because a schema document's location is the base URI its
    own relative references resolve against.
  - **`-q` is accepted and read by nothing**, and `TestValidateQuietDoesNotSuppress-
    Violations` compares the `-q` and non-`-q` reports byte for byte. `validate`
    has no informational output and `doc.go:41-43` forbids `-q` to silence its
    violations, so wiring it to anything would contradict the page (#472's
    settled scope). **`-v` is all or nothing** by a plain reference to
    `parse.go`'s existing `GAP(cmd)` marker (#1185) — no second marker, and no
    claim that `GOXSD_DEBUG` scopes anything.
  - **A stopped walk (`Result.Err`) is rendered beside the violations on stdout
    and counts as invalid** — nothing in the document was shown valid.
  - **README's exit-2 callout was EXTENDED, not duplicated** (*"narrowed when
    `parse` landed, and again when `validate` did"*), which is what the unblocking
    comment asked for and what `TestUsageCoversContract`'s seven new pins hold.
