## 2026-09-04 — post-land pass for #720

**ZERO unblocked and the zero is measured; BOTH arbiter follow-ups FILED —
#1223 and #1224 — and the pass's larger finding is that this landing falsified a
load-bearing sentence in FOUR open bodies, one of which (#755) it changed the
shape of entirely by shipping, inside a CLI slice, the very reader #755 exists to
find a home for.** #755's own Notes had predicted it in as many words —
*"a session taking #720 with this unfiled has to grow the reader inside a CLI
slice"* — so the correction is not a surprise being recorded, it is a prediction
being closed out, and the issue is now a **second-implementation** question with
two named divergences from its own Acceptance rather than a promise-versus-nothing
one. `docs/PLAN.md` is untouched: band row 4 is discharged, and cartographer
step 6 owns the Status section by replacement with no post-land variant, which is
what the landing entry's `Next` item 5 already said.

- **Unblocked: zero, measured, not assumed (#400).** All **252** open non-PR
  issues fetched over repo-scoped REST and paged explicitly to exhaustion
  (100 + 100 + 52, PRs filtered), then every body scanned for `#720` — **14**
  carry the string and exactly **one** carries it in a `## Depends on` section.
  None of the **20** open `blocked` bodies waits on this landing. #720 itself
  read `Depends on: none` from #472's post-land pass onward. **Zero relabelled**,
  and the measured zero is recorded on the thread
  (`issuecomment-5539050988`) as well as here.
- **#16 STAYS `blocked`, and the landing of both its named dependencies is
  exactly why that needed checking rather than assuming.** Its `## Depends on`
  named #472 (landed `69569fa`) and #720 (landed `f4d8f76`), and both were
  recorded there as **hand-offs rather than gates** because their criteria had
  been lifted out long before either landed. What holds it is the **`gen` (M9)**
  block it still carries against no filed subcommand issue — `gen`'s absent
  exit-code contract, `-backend`'s unstated default, `-out`'s unspecified
  directory behaviour — and its own `## Acceptance` (*"done when every criterion
  below has been lifted into a subcommand issue that closed"*) makes closing on
  the `validate` discharge a way of dropping those three on the floor. Its Notes
  forbid the relabel outright: *"Do not relabel it `ready`; fold slices out of it
  instead."* Disposition on the thread (`issuecomment-5539030398`); two stale
  premises corrected in the body, below.
- **#1223 filed** — `ready`, `kind/gap`, `area/cmd`, M5. The arbiter's follow-up
  1: the CLI drops `validate.Result.Unevaluated()`, so exit 0 means *no
  violation was charged* and an instance the assessment DECLINED to decide is
  byte-identical at the terminal to one that passed every check. Re-measured
  rather than copied from the verdict: `grep -rn Unevaluated cmd/` returns
  nothing at `f4d8f76`. The body leads with a **reachable-today witness** so the
  fix has a failing case — an `<xs:assert>` records under `cvc-assertion`
  (#719, `bd887cd`) and a declined Type Table under `key-cta-ta-select` (#56,
  `3160813`) — and admits **both** directions as closing it: surface the records,
  or state in the three contract copies that `validate` reports charged
  violations only. It pins the two settled points a fix must not re-open, `-q`'s
  scope (#1066) and the `0/1/2/3` table (#720). **Why it is a follow-up and not
  a blocker is recorded rather than re-argued**: no #720 Acceptance bullet asked
  for it and all three copies are honest today (`doc.go:41`, `main.go:62`,
  `README.md:73-75` all say *charged a violation*, never *valid*), so this is a
  missing capability and not a false sentence.
- **#1224 filed** — `blocked`, `kind/refactor`, `area/cmd`, M5. The arbiter's
  follow-up 2, marked *minor and optional* there: `hintFault`
  (`cmd/goxsd8/validate.go:287-293`) strips the location from **every** fault
  charged at `schemaSetLocation`, which is right only while every such message
  names the hinted document itself. `blocked` on a **trigger, not an issue** — a
  new rule becoming chargeable at the wrapper root — and the body states that
  **there is no honest startable-today half**: a test enumerating today's
  wrapper-root rules would be a fixture list that a newly added rule would not
  trip, which is the only case the issue exists for. It also records that the
  two available accounts of the reachable rule set **disagree on the second
  rule** — `hintFault`'s doc comment says `src-include` clause 2, the accept
  verdict says `src-import` against a non-well-formed document — and asks a taker
  to re-derive from `compileSet` rather than trust either.
- **Filed rather than handed off, deliberately (#330).** The verdict's own
  routing for follow-up 2 was *"worth a sentence on whichever issue next touches
  `compileSet`"*; no open issue mentions `compileSet`, `hintFault` or
  `schemaSetLocation` at all, so that hand-off named no ledger anyone could
  check. Both issues were created over repo-scoped REST and their bodies diffed
  against the files they were written from — **byte-identical modulo the
  auto-appended footer**, so #515's `<...>`-stripping hazard is measured clear
  rather than assumed.
- **FOUR open bodies corrected, not commented at.** Each carried a sentence this
  landing made false, and each sentence was load-bearing for a decision the
  issue asks a session to take:
  - **#398** — *"The list is now TWO names, not three"* is now **ONE**. `run`
    dispatches both `parse` and `validate` (`main.go:160-165`) and neither
    reaches `notImplementedFmt`, so the stub reserves only `gen`. The item's own
    argument for acting now — *"a marker over a shrinking stub… write it while
    two names are left"* — is **spent**, and the body now asks for that ruling
    explicitly (a marker over one reserved name may not earn its line; either
    answer closes the item). Its `TestUsageCoversContract` half is untouched.
  - **#1185** — *"`validate` and `codec` name packages no subcommand reaches
    yet… two names that can only be no-ops"* is false: `validate` injects into
    **both** packages the variable names, `validate.WithLogger`
    (`validate.go:159`, `:270`) and `parser.WithLogger` through `compileSet`
    (`:447`). The wire path now wires two real names and one no-op. The gap has
    a **second site** (`validate.go:121-124`) which points at the marker instead
    of duplicating it, so `gapaudit` still reads a `cmd` census of one and the

## 2026-09-04 — #999 — **`TestDiagnosesAreDistinct` is deleted, and the round-1 reject landed on the sentence written to justify the deletion — the one fault class this issue exists to close, installed in the tombstone for the test that carried it: "cannot fail on a classification collapse" is FALSE, and the arbiter falsified it with a third mutation nobody had named, reverting `diagnose` to its pre-#472 first-argument-only scan, which collapses two branches into `noSubcommand` — a bare constant with nothing to interpolate — so the deleted test's pairwise loop DID fire on it and was partially armed, not inert**: the deletion itself survives untouched, because `dispatchCases` catches that third mutation too, and what changed in the repair is the load-bearing justification a future reader has for the deletion being safe — narrowed to the collapses it actually holds for, the ones where both colliding branches interpolate the input argument (`notImplementedFmt`, `unknownSubcommandFmt`, `leadingFlagFmt`), with `noSubcommand` named as the collapse the deleted test stayed armed against and `dispatchCases` named as what still catches it; both mutations the issue names were **re-measured at HEAD** rather than carried from the body, which had already demoted them to hypotheses after #472 re-cut every line they name — `run()` skipping `diagnose()`, and the vocabulary lookup going case-insensitive (`slices.Contains` → `strings.EqualFold`, both call sites) — each applied to a scratch copy and run, each leaving the deleted test **green** and each failing `TestRunDispatch` and `TestBuiltBinaryMatrix`, the arbiter reproducing the whole matrix independently rather than reading mason's account of it; **`Ratchet: unchanged` is measured, not assumed** — `go list -deps ./conformance | grep -c cmd/goxsd8` is `0`, so no lane can see this package — `surface: unchanged` (`package main`, only `Test*` identifiers touched), **one file, +9/−24**; and the session's own process fact, disclosed on the thread rather than hidden or fabricated as mason's: **there was no mason round at all**, the orchestrating session held the pen for both commits.

  `main.go` is untouched and `dispatchCases` reproduces byte-for-byte, which is

- **Ratchet: unchanged** — the arbiter's own words at round 2, copied and not
  recomputed: **`RATCHET: unchanged`**, **`RATCHET-STATE: ratchet run, tree clean,

  because the verdict is reject`**, the standing rule (#303). The structural
  reason is measured at both rounds rather than inferred — `go list -deps
  ./conformance | grep -c cmd/goxsd8` is **`0`**. Gate all four parts green at both
  commits, the arbiter running them itself on the committed tree after its own
  submodule init: `go tool lint` **0 issues**, `commentwrap` clean with no reflow,
  `gofmt -l cmd/goxsd8/` clean, part 4 **PASS** (**387.13s** then **386.36s**),

- Decisions made and why:
  - **Deletion, not repair.** The issue's Acceptance offered both and named the
    condition for the second — *"if the test survives in some form, its comment
    states what it pins that `dispatchCases` does not"* — and after re-measurement
    there was nothing it pins that `dispatchCases` does not. `dispatchCases` is one
    encoding of the matrix driven twice, through `run` and through the built
    binary, and it drives every classification the deleted test named: `gen`,
    `frobnicate`, `-q`, `-q parse`, `-v gen`, `VALIDATE`.

    arbiter's reject said so in as many words — the deletion stands, what is false
    is the justification — so the repair is one comment hunk and no code.
    Restoring a test that is blind to both mutations it exists to catch, to keep a
    guard against a third that `dispatchCases` already catches, buys nothing and
    re-installs the defect.

    test disappeared, and its precision is what round 1 demanded. The one sentence
    dropped is the one that restated `dispatchCases`' own doc comment in substance
    (the arbiter's non-blocking note, CLAUDE.md Writing — state it once);

    itself.

    mason**, judging the change small, and disclosed it on the thread
    (`issuecomment-5541430047`) in place of a `MASON:` comment and labelled as the
    orchestrator's own, rather than composing an account in mason's name — which
    precondition 3 forbids outright (#565). See Friction: the disclosure's reading
    of the rules is itself wrong, and that is the part worth a retro.
- Surprises:
  - **The deleted test was partially armed, and the issue body is loose in exactly
    the same place the comment was.** *"Four values produced by four distinct
    format strings are unequal by construction"* is what the replacement comment
    inherited and generalized; it is false for any pair that can both return
    `noSubcommand`, which is a constant and not a format. A body sentence written
    as a standing truth propagated into a `.go` comment without either being
    measured.
  - **The mutation that armed the old test is the exact regression #472 landed to
    prevent** — the pre-#472 first-argument-only scan — so what the deleted test
    guarded was the one collapse the tree already has a landing against, while
    being blind to the two the issue was filed on.
  - **No `GROUNDING:` comment exists on this thread.** The body's own instruction
    — *"the two mutation OUTCOMES above are now hypotheses, not measurements. Re-run
    both at grounding before acting on either"* — was discharged, honestly and by
    running them, but the record of it landed inside the orchestrator's
    implementation account posted **after** the accept. The work happened; a cold
    reader looking for it where the body says to look finds nothing.
  - **The arbiter re-derived rather than reused.** At round 2 it rebuilt its own
    mutation harness and verified `main.pristine.go` matched the tree and
    `main_test.old.go` matched `origin/main` byte-for-byte before trusting either,
    then re-ran all three mutations. Round 1's finding came out of running a
    mutation the actor had not run; round 2's accept came out of not trusting
    round 1's own scaffolding.
- Friction — **two, and the first is a recurrence with a ruling already pointed at
  it.** (1) *A false producer-mechanism sentence written into a `.go` comment,
  rejected at round 1 by an arbiter mutation* is the family #1199 closed on
  2026-09-03 having ruled **no new rule is warranted**, and that ruling named its
  own reopening condition: *"a landing rejected round-1 for a false
  producer-mechanism sentence written into a comment while its issue body already
  carried a probe note."* This landing is the closest thing to it since, and
  whether it FIRES is a real judgment rather than a reading: the body did carry a
  probe note, but that note bound the **two named mutation outcomes** and the false
  sentence generalized past them onto a third mutation nobody had named, inheriting
  the body's own unprobed *"by construction"* clause instead. Recorded, not ruled —
  the ruling puts this on `/retro` and on **#642**, which is where a general rule
  would be written and which is explicitly free to overturn #1199 on its own
  evidence. It does **not** extend the narrower n=2 #720 banded — the blocking
  finding being an item the actor's own *"scrutinize this hardest"* list had
  named and declined to prove — because no such list and no pre-verdict account
  existed here; that band stays at 2. (2) *The orchestrator skipped the mason delegation*,
  and the disclosure comment's own diagnosis — *"nothing in `/develop` or WORKFLOW
  currently stops the orchestrating session from skipping the mason delegation on a
  change it judges small"* — **is falsified twice over**: `docs/WORKFLOW.md`'s pen
  rule (#649) already bounds it — *"the orchestrator holds the pen itself only when
  the edit carries no design content and its scope is provable: a change a review
  verdict specifies verbatim, or text no compiler reads. Everything else is a mason
  round"* — and `/develop` step 4 says *"then delegate to mason, always with
  worktree isolation"* with no exception at all. Judged against that bound, the two
  commits split: **`d9e682d` is inside it** (comment-only, and specified by the
  verdict), **`9f0f836` is not** (deleting a `func Test…` is code the compiler
  reads, and choosing deletion over repair is the design content of this issue).
  So the gap is not a missing rule but a missing **check**: precondition 3 binds on
  *"every mason commit on the branch"*, and a branch with no mason commits satisfies
  it vacuously — which is what happened here, since the honest disclosure that
  replaced the `MASON:` comment is also what let the precondition pass. That is
  n=2 with #636 (#649's case, where the same shortcut then produced an outright
  amend-and-force-push) and it is banded for `/retro`, not filed (PRINCIPLES 27).

## 2026-09-04 — #1215 — **`src-element` clause 4 (`ed-with-ns`) goes from charged nowhere to charged on the inline `name=` local element alone, and the `schema` lane banks 13928 → 13932 (+4) — ONE PAST the queue's own ceiling of three, because the fourth flip is `saxonData/TargetNS/target002.n.xsd`, plain UTF-8, and one whole-suite grep for an `<element>` carrying `targetNamespace` returns it**: the executable change is `(*producer).rejectLocalElementTargetNamespace`, wired into `produceLocalElement` behind `checkS4SChildOrder` and ahead of `declarationName`, charging 4.1 (name present), 4.2 (form absent) and 4.3's ancestor test through two new helpers — `nearestComplexTypeAndRestriction`, one upward walk over `Element.parent` returning at the first `<complexType>` and carrying the innermost `<restriction>` seen below it, and `(*producer).baseMatchesAnyType`, 4.3.2's ·match· (`key-en-match`, `xmlschema11-1.md:2726`) asked of the `<restriction>` and bound against ITS in-scope namespaces (PRINCIPLES 19), never the element's; **4.3's antecedent carries the clause's whole force** and is read first, so an element writing its own document's `targetNamespace` redundantly stays legal wherever it stands, while 4.3.1 and 4.3.2 being conjoined makes an `<extension>` between the element and the nearest `<complexType>`, or a `<restriction base="xs:anyType">`, fail as squarely as no `<complexType>` ancestor at all; the ancestor `<schema>`'s namespace is read as the EFFECTIVE one (`p.target`, presence via `p.chameleon()`) so §F.1 task (a)'s chameleon coercion is honoured and an `<override>`-substituted declaration is measured against the overridden document's defaults (PRINCIPLES 16) — the one line mason named as the whole disagreement if the arbiter read §4.2.5 the other way, and the arbiter ruled it correct as written; the issue's CONDITIONAL case was settled by MEASUREMENT and not by report — the arbiter ran `go run ./cmd/goxsd8 parse` itself and the clause-4 verdict lands at `s3_2_3si01.xsd:11:4`, ahead in document order of the `src-attribute` clause 6.3.2 fault at line 22 that #1216 owns, so this first-error-wins producer reports the element side and **#1216 must predict `schema +0` for `s3_2_3si01`**; the arbiter **ACCEPTED at round 1 with ZERO findings**, replaying both mutation claims here rather than accepting them (neutering the call fails 8 of 8 rejection rows and 0 of 3 acceptance rows; binding `base=` at `el.parent` fails exactly the prefix-rebinding row) and restoring the tree byte-identical to `55c0d93` before the ratchet run — `Ratchet: schema +4`, `surface: unchanged`, **3 files, +371/−6**.

- Decisions made and why:
  - **The ancestor question is answered on the XML tree, not on the component
    graph.** Clause 4.3.1/4.3.2 are stated over XML *ancestors*, so a
    `<restriction>` reached through a named `<group>` reference would answer
    differently under the component reading — and no component of any ancestor
    exists while a local element is being mapped. One `Element.parent` walk is
    decidable at exactly the point the clause is charged.
  - **`p.target`, not the ancestor `<schema>`'s raw attribute.** The two genuinely
    diverge under `<override>`: the XML ancestor `<schema>` of a substituted
    declaration is the *overriding* document's. PRINCIPLES 16 settles it — the
    declaration takes the overridden document's schema-level defaults — and
    `localTargetNS` already mints the element's own namespace on that same
    footing, so reading the constraint on the other one would let the constraint
    and the mapping disagree about a single element. `foldDefaultAttributes` and
    `defaultOpenContentElem` take this reading too; `licensedNamespace` takes the
    other one only because src-resolve clause 4.1.1 asks for the *attribute* by
    name.
  - **Presence stays a distinct fact from an empty value.** `own || p.chameleon()`
    is the presence test, so a schema with no `targetNamespace` at all satisfies
    4.3's antecedent whatever the element wrote — pinned by a
    `targetNamespace=""` row that no other row would catch.
  - **Charging 4.1 is a sanctioned absorb, not creep.** The arbiter put it to
    `docs/WORKFLOW.md`'s scope rule and it answers no to all three questions: the
    grounding covers clause 4 entire including 4.1; no exported surface; and it
    cannot move a lane on its own, since both the old `e-props-correct` clause 1
    diagnostic and the new 4.1 one reject. A helper documented as "clause 4" that
    silently skipped 4.1 would be a comment asserting more than the code does. It
    is named in the commit body as the rule requires.
  - **The innermost `<restriction>`, on a reachability argument the arbiter
    checked rather than took.** `<restriction>` occurs only under
    `<complexContent>`, `<simpleContent>` or `<simpleType>`, each a child of a
    `<complexType>` or `<simpleType>`; reaching a second one while walking up
    from an `<element>` needs an intervening `<complexType>` (which ends the
    walk) or `<simpleType>` (which admits no `<element>` below it). At most one
    can stand between an element and its nearest `<complexType>`, so 4.3.2's
    existential reading and this innermost one pick the same element and the
    choice is unobservable.
  - **`baseMatchesAnyType` answers `false` for an absent or unbindable `base=`,
    and swallows nothing.** `produceComplexContent` charges the missing-`base`
    grammar fault and resolves `base=` at `produce_complex.go:934-946`, both
    BEFORE `complexContentType` descends into the content model that reaches the
    only caller here. `bindQName` rather than `resolveQName` is deliberate:
    re-asking the src-resolve clause 4 licensing question at a second position
    would double-charge it.
  - **Two stale doc comments corrected in the same commit**, the scope rule's own
    worked example. `produceLocalElement`'s doc asserted *"the ancestor axis is
    not walkable from an `*Element`"*, which the retained tree's parent link had
    already made false — the real reason `scopeParent` is a parameter is that the
    axis reaches the ancestor's ELEMENT, never the component, which does not
    exist until the caller finishes building it. `rejectRefElementDeclarationAttrs`'s
    doc ended *"Clauses 2.1 and 4 are charged nowhere in this producer"*;
    narrowed to 2.1 alone, with a pointer to where clause 4 now lives and why a
    `ref=` form never reaches it. **Clause 2.1 is still charged nowhere, and
    stays so after this landing.**
- Surprises:
  - **The suite ships the negative control next door to the positive one, and the
    branch got both right.** `saxonData/TargetNS/target001.xsd` and
    `target002.n.xsd` are byte-for-byte the same schema apart from
    `<xs:restriction base="B">` versus `<xs:extension base="B">` — one `valid`,
    one `invalid`. target001 was `pass` and stayed `pass`; target002 flipped.
    That pair is the STYLE E2 evidence in its strongest available form: the
    suite's own metadata for target002 reads *"Invalid because not part of an
    xs:restriction"* (`saxonMeta/TargetNS.testSet:52`) and the charge fires at
    `target002.n.xsd:12:12` citing exactly 4.3.2. A false-positive on 4.3's
    antecedent, or on the restriction/extension distinction, would have shown up
    as a regression on target001 in the same run.
  - **`produceLocalElement` now charges `src-element` on BOTH sides of the s4s
    walk, and #1136's thesis has acquired a counter-example inside one
    function.** #1136 is filed on the producers disagreeing with each other —
    *"two charge `src-element` BEFORE `checkS4SChildOrder` and
    `elementParticleTerm` charges AFTER"* — and its evidence table's
    `produceLocalElement` row lists clause 3 before the walk. Clause 4 is now
    charged after it, deliberately (grammar faults answer first, matching
    `elementParticleTerm`, and 4.1 must answer before `declarationName` reports
    the absent name it implies). The non-uniformity is no longer only between
    functions.
  - **`nilerr` caught a real defect en route.** Mason's first shape had
    `if err != nil { return nil }` on the `base=` binding; gate part 2 flagged
    it, and the fix was to move the question into `baseMatchesAnyType`, which
    returns the boolean the clause actually asks for, rather than to restructure
    around the linter.
  - **The `ibmData/S3_2_3` fixture paths are not the ones the test-set metadata
    names.** The three files sit flat under `ibmData/schema_invalid/S3_2_3/`, not
    at the `.../schema/...s` path the metadata gives; the oracle reconciled them
    by case name against `schema.txt:15150,15155,15157` rather than by path.
- Friction — **the predicted figure was derived from one directory, and the case
  that beat it was one grep away in another.** The band row and the issue body
  both name three fixtures, all in `ibmData/schema_invalid/S3_2_3/`, and the
  grounding round verified those three exist rather than re-taking the census;
  `grep -rlE '<(xs:|xsd:)?element[^>]*targetNamespace' --include='*.xsd'` over
  the whole suite returns two files, neither of them in `ibmData`, and one of
  them is `target002.n.xsd`. **The last stamp's standing instruction —
  *"Decode `ibmData/` before grounding anything against the suite"* — fixed the
  DECODER and left the SCOPE unfixed**, and the miss it did not cover came from

- Decisions made and why:
  - **Clause 6.1 is charged NOWHERE, and the `ref=` form that would fail it is a
    marked gap rather than a folded-in charge.** The grounding ruled 6.1's one
    reachable failure to be the `ref=` path — 3.1 forces `name` absent whenever
    `ref` is present — and that path returns from `produceAttributeUse` ahead of
    this function. Charging it here was refused on `docs/WORKFLOW.md`'s scope
    rule as the arbiter applied it: the form **can move `schema` on its own**, so
    folding it in would blur the +5 this branch banks (PRINCIPLES 22).
  - **The 3.2-versus-2.2 asymmetry is real and immaterial, and the issue body
    overstated it.** The grounding corrected the body's *"2.2 DOES name
    targetNamespace explicitly"*: `src-element` clause 2.2 is a **blanket**
    exclusion (`xmlschema11-1.md:1321`) that sweeps `targetNamespace` in by
    omission from an allowlist, while `src-attribute` clause 3.2 names
    `<simpleType>`, `form` and `type` and stops. The attribute side has no
    element-side twin for the `ref=` form, which is exactly why that gap exists
    on this side and not on #1215's.
  - **6.2 before 6.3, and the producer owns the order.** Clause 6's conjuncts are
    unordered, both faults carry `ruleSrcAttribute`, and no verdict is fabricated
    either way; the arbiter read no precedence into §B. The order is what makes
    `s3_2_3si03` attributable to the clause its own fixture comment names, which
    is a point in its favour rather than a tiebreak.
  - **`GAP(xsd)`, not `GAP(parser)`, and the split is by SUBJECT not by
    directory.** `parser/` carries 9 `xsd`, 3 `parser` and 2 `xml` markers
    (excluding the stale `.claude/worktrees/` copies that inflate any tree-wide
    count) and the arbiter read every one: `GAP(xsd)` marks a spec constraint
    this producer charges incompletely (`:489`, `:2072`, `:3590`,
    `redefine.go:401`, three in `doc.go`), `GAP(parser)` a parser MECHANISM gap
    (`conditional.go:208`, `doc.go:89`). Two partial charges of a representation
    constraint are `:2072`'s shape exactly.
  - **Both markers are BARE — no `#N` at all — and that is the finished state,
    not a deferral.** STYLE P3 permits the ownerless form; a marker pointing at
    the issue the landing closes is the dead end P3's closing sentence names.
    Mason searched for a real open owner and found only #1216 itself; the
    arbiter re-ran the search and agreed, and `gapaudit` against the live
    262-issue feed puts both in **group 1** carrying only heuristic
    `file-matches` annotations with **no `dead end` row**, which is the correct
    resting state for a bare marker.
  - **The test comment's `GAP(xsd)` back-reference is deliberately COLONLESS**,
    so `gapaudit`'s `markerPattern` does not read it as a second marker site and
    the record stays single-sited. Seven in-tree precedents for the form.
  - **Three stale doc claims absorbed** rather than filed, the scope rule's own
    worked example, all falsified by this diff: `produceLocalAttribute`'s *"the
    ancestor axis is not walkable from an `*Element`"* (already false once #1215
    landed the walk, and this call site walks it from inside that very
    function), `nearestComplexTypeAndRestriction`'s src-element-only clause list,

## 2026-09-04 — post-land pass for #1216

**ZERO unblocked and the zero is measured; both `GAP(xsd)` markers this landing
created are FILED rather than left bare — #1242 (`ref=`) and #1243
(`use="prohibited"`) — which is this pass's one real judgment call and the
opposite of #1215's, where nothing needed filing. The census-scope defect's THIRD
sighting went to #1239 as corroboration and, because that body's *"two
sightings"* framing was understating the evidence a taker must satisfy, its title
and Acceptance were corrected rather than only commented on. #1136 took the
attribute-side run-order instance as Notes-level BLAST RADIUS and NOT as a fourth
evidence row, which is a ruling the landing entry explicitly declined to make.
`docs/PLAN.md` is again declined ON THE RULE, third pass running.**

- **Unblocked: zero, measured, not assumed (#400).** All **261** open non-PR
  issues fetched over repo-scoped REST and paged explicitly to exhaustion
  (100 + 100 + 61, PRs filtered; `--paginate` is unusable here — its Link headers
  point at `repositories/{id}` and the proxy 403s them, so the page-numbered
  recipe in `docs/ROUTINES.md` is the working channel). Every body scanned:
  **`att-with-ns` appears in ZERO of the 261**, and `#1216` in zero — the two
  carrying `#1215` are **#1239** and **#1136**, both `ready`, both cross-
  references rather than dependencies. The `## Depends on` section of every one
  of the **21** open `blocked` issues was printed and read; **none names #1216,
  #1215, `src-attribute clause 6` or `att-with-ns`**, and **ten** of the 21 are
  triggers rather than issues and say so in their own words (#79, #250, #555,
  #692, #841, #925, #1002, #1080, #1220, #1224) — do not re-scan those on the
  next sweep. #1216 itself read `Depends on: none`. **Zero relabelled.**
- **Follow-up 1 — the two boundary gaps — BOTH FILED, and this is the pass's one
  contestable call.** The landing entry handed them over explicitly (*"the
  post-land cartographer pass's to file or to rule deliberately unowned in
  writing"*), and the case for leaving them bare was real: STYLE P3 permits the
  ownerless form, `gapaudit` already reports both correctly, and both are
  obscure corner cases with no known fixture. **Filed anyway, on three grounds.**
  (a) A `/backlog` run's step 3 files `kind/gap` issues for untracked GAP sites,
  so these get filed eventually by construction — and filing them now, while the
  GROUNDING, the arbiter's CLI reproduction and the `prohibitedAttributeNames`
  finding are all one thread away, is strictly cheaper than a future pass
  re-deriving them cold. (b) The arbiter deferred both to *a separate issue*
  rather than to nothing, on `docs/WORKFLOW.md`'s scope rule — *"both boundaries
  can move `schema`"* — so an issue is the deferral's other half. (c) #1102 is
  the standing precedent one package over: an unowned `GAP(xsd)` is filed to get
  an owner AND a ruling. **Filed APART, not together**, on the discipline #471
  set and #1205/#1206/#1215/#1216 kept: different arms of `produceAttributeUse`,
  different clauses, and only one requires moving a call site.
  - **#1242** (`ready`, `kind/gap`, `area/parser`, M4) — `produce_complex.go:3107`,
    a local `attribute ref=` writing `targetNamespace`. **Clause 6.1's one
    reachable failure anywhere**, and the body says why that does not reopen
    #1216's dead-code ruling: 3.1 forces `name` absent whenever `ref` is present
    and 3.2's forbidden list stops at `simpleType`/`form`/`type`, so 6.1 binds
    and fails on the arm #1216 never served. Carries the element-side asymmetry
    (#1206's `rejectRefElementDeclarationAttrs` charges the twin under clause
    2.2's BLANKET exclusion, which `src-attribute` has no equivalent of) and
    names `TestProduceRefAttributeTargetNamespaceStaysAccepted` as the issue's
    own inverse — leaving it green is proof the charge did not land.
  - **#1243** (same labels and milestone) — `produce_complex.go:3120`, an
    `attribute use="prohibited"`. Carries **both** facts the thread holds and the
    marker deliberately does not (STYLE P3a): closing it **reorders clause 6
    against clause 4** (`:3206` against `:3202`), and `prohibitedAttributeNames`
    (`:2705`) DOES read the `targetNamespace` at `:2726` through `localTargetNS`,
    so the end-to-end consequence is a research task and not an obvious silent
    drop. It also records that **this gap is pinned by nothing** — the round-2
    verdict's CLI run is its only reproduction anywhere — and asks a taker to
    re-derive it before implementing.
  - **NEITHER body predicts a ratchet figure, on purpose.** Both carry the
    census-by-CONSTRUCT instruction and point at #1239. Predicting `+0` here is
    the exact move #1206's landing falsified when `docs/PLAN.md` had dismissed a
    tracker for *"zero suite fixtures"* that turned out to be a UTF-16LE artefact.
  - **Marker repointing is NAMED, not done.** Both markers still read *"No open
    issue owns retiring it"*. Each new issue's Acceptance makes retiring its own
    marker part of that landing, so the loop closes there; the one-line edit is
    absorbable by any session touching `produce_complex.go` and is not worth a
    session. Bare-and-`gapaudit`-visible is the honest interim state.
- **Follow-up 2 — `docs/PLAN.md` band rows 1 and 2 — NOT EDITED, on the rule, for
  the THIRD consecutive pass.** Both #1215 and #1216 have landed and the band
  still carries them at *"+2 firm plus conditional"* and *"+3, all firm"*, the
  second now wrong twice over since the landing banked +5. It stays until the
  next `/backlog`. Cartographer step 6 owns that section **by replacement**, a
  spot edit to one row is the thing it forbids, and there is no post-land variant
  of the rule; #999's and #1215's post-land passes declined the identical edit for
  the identical reason. **Not a hand-off in #330's sense, and the difference is
  mechanical**: the Status section is date-stamped and rewritten wholesale from
  `lanestatus` and GitHub every stamp, so a closed issue cannot survive in it —
  there is a ledger, and it is the section's own replacement discipline. A
  *whole-section* replacement was considered and refused on inputs rather than on
  authority: step 6's deliverable is a dependency-ordered band, which needs
  `wipsurvey`, `gapaudit` and the persona fold a single-issue pass does not run,
  and publishing a band this pass has not earned is worse than publishing a stale
  one that self-corrects. The landing entry had already ruled it *"discharged by
  the next `/backlog`'s whole-section replacement and not by a word-level edit"*.
- **Follow-up 3 — #1239's THIRD sighting — POSTED, and the BODY corrected too**
  (`issuecomment-5547017446`). The reading was checked before it was endorsed:
  #1239's Goal is *"which fixtures carry construct X"*, and a construct-keyed
  census returns `s3_2_3si03` — so this sighting is the **first the filed design
  answers outright with no redesign**, which corroborates the instrument rather
  than widening it. Sightings 1 and 2 each demanded something the previous fix
  lacked (a decoder, then a scope); this one demands nothing new, and that is the
  strongest evidence yet that the tool is the right shape. **Verified rather than
  transcribed** — unlike #1239's filing container, this one had
  `testdata/xsdtests` initialized, so `file` was run on
  `ibmData/schema_invalid/S3_2_3/s3_2_3si03.xsd` (`Unicode text, UTF-16,
  little-endian`) and its trailing comment read from the decoded bytes
  (*"Structures section 3.2.3.6.2 … The form attribute is present when
  targetNamespace attribute is present"*), confirming both the encoding and the
  clause attribution first-hand. **The body was corrected and not only
  commented on**, per WORKFLOW's filing discipline: the title now says **THREE**
  landings and names all three mechanisms with *"the fix installed for each does
  not reach the next"*; sighting 3 is written into the sightings section; and
  Acceptance gains a **second acceptance case** which is the one that constrains
  the query VOCABULARY rather than its reach — si03 differs from si02/si05/si09
  only in WHICH CLAUSE it violates, so an interface whose natural query is a
  clause shape rather than a construct misses it exactly as the human greps did,
  and would ship having reproduced the defect while passing case (1). **#679
  stays open and separate** (a *grounding's* scope exclusion, not an issue body's
  own enumeration); neither is blocked on the other.
- **Follow-up 4 — #1136's attribute-side instance — FOLDED IN as `## Notes`
  blast radius, NOT as a fourth evidence row** (`issuecomment-5547027353`). The

## 2026-09-05 — #1229 — **README stops claiming that a hint naming a document that is NOT THERE is reported on stderr, and the whole landing is one README paragraph — the two contract copies disagreed, `cmd/goxsd8/doc.go` was the true one, and the shipped behaviour was already pinned by a test**: Acceptance branch **A** (correct README), no production change, **ARBITER ACCEPT round 1 with zero findings**, `Ratchet: unchanged` run rather than inferred, `surface: unchanged`, **one file, +10/−6**. The grounding ruled BOTH branches spec-conformant and therefore ruled the choice out of the spec's hands — `src-import` (*"It is not an error for the application schema component reference strategy to fail"*) and `src-include` (*"It is not an error for the ·actual value· of the `schemaLocation` attribute to fail to resolve at all, in which case the corresponding inclusion must not be performed"*) make silent degradation legal, while §4.2.6.2's closing note (*"perhaps calling for a processor-dependent error should the URI fail to resolve"*) makes reporting legal too, so what was left was a goxsd8 contract-consistency call and the cheap correct one won. **Branch B was declined on a mechanism mason and the arbiter each read for themselves, not on cost alone**: `validatorFor` hands `compileSet` the `-schema` documents and the instance's hints as ONE wrapper root (`append(slices.Clone(vn.docs), found...)`, `cmd/goxsd8/validate.go:433-448`), so `AssemblyReport.Unfollowed()` comes back with a `-schema` document's own unresolvable `<xs:include>` mixed in with the hints — reporting it wholesale changes the contract for the `-schema` set as well, and filtering to hint-contributed entries is new attribution logic on the `hintFault` path **#1224** is already editing.

- **Ratchet: unchanged — every lane**, the arbiter's figures copied, not

- Cost — **1 oracle, 1 mason, 1 arbiter.** Zero repair rounds, zero rejects, no

## 2026-09-05 — post-land pass for #1229

**ZERO unblocked, measured two ways over all 263 open issues; BOTH follow-ups
this landing left are filed — #1250 (sibling-poisoning, the arbiter's
declined finding) and #1251 (the declined "or report it too" acceptance
branch, the call the landing entry assigned to this pass) — and each was
reproduced against the built binary rather than transcribed. The reproduction
paid for itself twice: #1250 gains a FOURTH row the thread never measured, a
NOT-WELL-FORMED sibling poisoning its siblings exactly as a mis-paired one
does, which reclassifies the divergence from "src-import clause 3.1 versus
missing" to "any fault that stops the wrapper compiling versus a directive
that resolves to nothing"; and #1251's cost is WORSE than the decline
recorded, because `UnfollowedDirective` carries `Reason` and `At` only — no
`schemaLocation` value — and `schemaSetSource` writes the whole wrapper on
ONE line. Neither is `blocked` on #1224, and that is this pass's one real
judgment call.**

- **Unblocked: zero, measured, not assumed (#400).** All **263** open non-PR
  issues fetched over repo-scoped REST, paged explicitly to exhaustion and
  reshaped per `docs/ROUTINES.md`'s **Survey input** recipe (`gh issue list`
  403s here — *"only the pinned set of PR-review operations is served"* — and
  `--paginate` walks into the numeric-ID Link headers, both exactly as the
  last three passes recorded). **Two measurements.** (a) The `## Depends on`
  section of every one of the **21** open `blocked` issues was printed and
  read: **none names #1229**, and `1229` appears in **zero of the 21
  bodies**. (b) A full-body scan of all 263: `#1229` appears in exactly
  **one**, **#1233**, which is already `ready` and cites it as a neighbouring
  contract-copy defect, not a gate. **Zero relabelled.** The zero was
  predicted — #1229 read `Depends on: none` and named #1224/#1223 as
  sequence-related — and a doc-only landing that changed no exported surface
  and no production line can gate nothing by construction. The prediction is
  not the measurement; the sweep ran.
- **Follow-up 1 — sibling-poisoning — FILED as #1250** (`ready`, `kind/bug`,
  `area/cmd`, `area/docs`, M5). The arbiter surfaced it as *"not a finding —
  worth its own issue"* and ruled it *"a behaviour question before it is a
  prose question"*; this pass reproduced it rather than quoting it, building
  `./cmd/goxsd8` at `20010ed` and running four instances against one
  `-schema` set that declares nothing for their root, so **exit 0 can only
  mean the good hint applied**:

  | second pair in `xsi:schemaLocation` | exit | stderr |
  |---|---|---|
  | *(none — control)* | 0 | empty |
  | names a MISSING document | 0 | empty |
  | MIS-PAIRED namespace | 1 | `[src-import] … clause 3.1 …` |
  | NOT WELL-FORMED | 1 | `[src-import] … [xml-wf] unexpected EOF` |

  - **Row 4 is new and it moves the issue's framing.** The thread measured
    the mis-paired case alone, which leaves open the reading that
    `src-import` clause 3.1 is what poisons siblings. It is not: a malformed
    sibling poisons identically, so the real line is *any fault that stops
    the single wrapper root compiling* (set-wide) versus *a directive that
    resolves to nothing* (per-hint, and not a fault at all under
    `src-import`/`src-include`). A session grounding this now has the right
    question in front of it.
  - **`kind/bug`, not `kind/gap`.** `kind/gap` tracks `GAP(` and fail-open
    sites (`docs/WORKFLOW.md`), and this is neither. Both contract copies say
    something measurably FALSE once two hints are present —
    `README.md:109-117` and `doc.go:129-136` call the missing case *"degrades
    the same way"* / *"already degrades"* when it drops nothing and does not
    fall back to the `-schema` set alone — which is STYLE T6, the same rule
    #1229 charged.
  - The body records **why the compression survived #1229's judgment**: the
    single-hint framing both paragraphs use makes it true, and the arbiter's
    reasons for not charging it are quoted rather than re-argued. It also
    records that **nothing pins multi-hint behaviour today** —
    `validate_test.go:281`'s `instance` helper builds exactly one pair — so
    either branch owes a new test beside it, not an edit to it.
- **Follow-up 2 — the declined "or report it too" branch — FILED as #1251**
  (`ready`, `kind/gap`, `area/cmd`, `area/docs`, M5). The landing entry's
  Next item 3 left this call to this pass, with both halves of the argument
  on the thread. **Filed, and on the merits**: it is a cliuser finding with
  an operator-facing symptom (a typo'd `schemaLocation` costs exit 1 with a
  0-byte stderr), the grounding ruled it spec-legal, and the decline was
  explicitly deferral rather than refusal. `kind/gap` follows #1223, the same
  shape one layer up — `validate` holding information it never surfaces.
  - **Two facts checked here that make the decline's cost estimate too
    kind**, both recorded in the body so no session re-derives them: `go doc
    ./parser UnfollowedDirective` is `Reason UnfollowedReason` and `At
    xsderr.Loc` and **nothing else** — no `schemaLocation` value and no
    namespace — so the hinted location the diagnosis must name is not in the
    report at all and has to come from the CLI's own `[]schemaDoc`; and
    `schemaSetSource` (`validate.go:459-471`) writes the entire wrapper on
    **one line**, so position-based attribution inside it is column-only.
    Whether `At` is usable for attribution at all is left OPEN and marked
    open, not ruled.
  - **The mixed-report argument is refined, not repeated.** A `-schema`
    document's *own* unresolvable `<xs:include>` IS separable — its `At.URI`
    is that document's real path, not `schemaSetLocation`. What is not
    separable by `At.URI` is a `-schema` argument's wrapper directive from a
    hint's, since both live in the wrapper root. That is the residue of
    mason's and the arbiter's reading, stated precisely enough to design
    against.

  deliberately.** Both touch `hintFault`'s lines and the landing entry says
  the work *"sequences after #1224"*. But #1224's own `## Depends on` is a
  **trigger** — a new rule chargeable at `schemaSetLocation` — that may never
  fire, so gating on it would park a persona-sourced operator gap and a false
  contract behind an issue nothing can start. Both are `ready` with the
  sequence recorded as adjacency, exactly as #1229 itself recorded the same

- **Nothing else was outstanding.** The landing entry's Next items 1 and 4
